## 110. [STYLIZED FACTS OF BITCOIN AND ETHEREUM RETURNS: HEAVY TAILS, VOLATILITY PERSISTENCE AND LONG MEMORY](<https://doi.org/10.22190/fueo260831016r>)

- 作者：Ognjen Radović; Jelena Radojičić
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.22190/fueo260831016r
- [打开 PDF](<https://casopisi.junis.ni.ac.rs/index.php/FUEconOrg/article/download/15231/6185>)

**摘要：**

This paper documents the stylized facts of daily Bitcoin (BTC) and Ethereum (ETH) returns and examines whether estimates of tail thickness, volatility persistence and long memory are mutually consistent. The main sample covers 3,212 days (November 2017–August 2026) for both assets, with BTC data from 2016 as a robustness check. The analysis combines unit-root tests, Hill tail estimation, robust portmanteau tests, GARCH-type models, temporal aggregation and detrended fluctuation analysis (DFA). Returns are stationary, negatively skewed and leptokurtic, with Hill tail indices of 2.5–3.3, suggesting a finite variance but an infinite fourth moment. Once heteroskedasticity is accounted for, returns show no robust linear autocorrelation, whereas absolute returns remain autocorrelated beyond 50 lags. Full-sample GARCH(1,1)-t persistence lies on the integrated boundary, but after the Ethereum Merge it falls to 0.92 for BTC and 0.84 for ETH and integrated GARCH is rejected for ETH in both subsamples. Together with a crossover in the DFA scaling of absolute returns at approximately two months, these findings indicate that apparent integrated volatility reflects regime shifts. GARCH asymmetry terms are insignificant and monthly aggregation removes non-normality for ETH but not for BTC. Full-sample persistence and Hurst estimates should therefore not be treated as structural parameters of cryptocurrency risk models.

---

## 112. [Sigelith Hourglass: 30 Days of Measurements of a Proof of Minimum Elapsed Time Based on Sequential SHA-256 Work](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24460342>)

- 作者：Adam Koch
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24460342

**摘要：**

Hourglass (Polish: Klepsydra) is a service that continuously computes consecutive segments of a proof of sequential work following the construction of Cohen and Pietrzak, using only the SHA-256 hash function. The seed of every segment absorbs the root of the previous segment, a fresh Bitcoin block and the latest checkpoint of the public Sigelith log, and the record of every segment is timestamped in that log. This yields a proof of minimum elapsed time: under the explicit assumption that no one computes this structure more than k times faster than our server, at least as much time as follows from the work done elapsed between an event absorbed into a seed and a file that contains a later root. This note describes 30 days of measurements on a production server (19 October to 18 November 2026): 368 segments, three planned interruptions lasting 36.8 s in total, and all records timestamped and checked with an independent reference implementation. The computing speed was stable over the day but changed twice in steps (−6.7% and +2.1%) without any change on our side. Assuming k = 20, thirty days of work prove at least 25.6 h, about 1/28 of real time. The note justifies k from the literature and states openly what is not yet known. Files: Sigelith\_Hourglass\_note1\_v1.0\_EN.pdf (English translation), Sigelith\_Klepsydra\_notatka1\_v1.0\_PL.pdf (Polish original) and sigelith-hourglass-phase0-dataset-v1.0.zip (phase 0 dataset: genesis, 368 segment records with proofs and stamp receipts, per-minute metrics, verifier output and analysis scripts; README.md inside explains how to check the whole chain). The SHA-256 fingerprints of the three files were timestamped in the public Sigelith log on 23 November 2026 - entry no. 1345 (English), entry no. 1346 (Polish) and entry no. 1347 (dataset):https://sigelith.org/proof/?h=f8476ffa0d91f24ea95c12e47cda53c18c966f8e52aaa1eb9167437762a1823chttps://sigelith.org/proof/?h=6e6eb94a06fb960e6fb0a2f1aeb4c6f98deeadbeb81f46c348862a0b40628f14https://sigelith.org/proof/?h=44650033253ec93444970a891c5c8ab96a8ee8babff55b37121459b47af8f743

## 114. [Enigma Academy: Courses, Examinations and Verifiable Certificates inside a Messenger](<https://doi.org/10.66693/iaic.tr.2026.4>)

- 作者：Andrey Vitalievich Nechesov
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.66693/iaic.tr.2026.4
- [打开 PDF](<https://enigma.ist/downloads/reports/iaic/2026/4/IAIC-TR-2026-04.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Enigma Academy is the learning layer of the Enigma messenger: courses for school and university subjects that run inside chats, assessment that is checked by the machine wherever a machine can decide and by a named examiner where it cannot, and certificates that anyone can verify without trusting the platform. A course is an application on the messenger's application interface, in the manner of messenger bots with inline buttons. The interface is general: any other service can be built on it, and courses are its first use. Problems are generated from templates whose answers a computer algebra system computes, so every variant is checked automatically and every learner receives fresh problems; artificial intelligence explains and hints within the learner's plan, and never awards a result on its own. The report specifies the roles; the application interface; courses, problem templates and the checking modes; examinations, recorded attempts and video answers reviewed by examiners; three levels of certificate; the learner's personal record chain, its daily commitment to the Enigma Root and the Root's anchor in Bitcoin; presentation of a certificate signed with the learner's key, with no name of a learner or an examiner written on any chain; protection of minors and the data policy; plans and limits; security; and the implementation status. No coin, token or fee on the chain is involved.

## 115. [On-Chain Determinants of Bitcoin Realized Volatility: A Structural-Break-Aware Forecasting Approach](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11806418/v1>)

- 作者：HAN GAO; PEI SHAN FAM
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11806418/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11806418/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This study examines whether on-chain activity metrics provide incremental information for forecasting Bitcoin realized volatility beyond the benchmark HAR-RV model. Using daily data from August 2017 to May 2026, a Garman-Klass range-based realized volatility measure is constructed. A dominant structural break on 1 October 2022 partitions the sample into two regimes; a secondary May 2018 break is retained as a robustness diagnostic. LASSO-regularized HAR-RV estimation with signed semivariance raises in-sample R² from 0.43 to 0.51 (pre-2022) and 0.20 to 0.43 (post-2022). In a real-time rolling out-of-sample design with lagged on-chain predictors, the augmented model significantly outperforms the benchmark in both periods (DM = 2.79, p = 0.005; DM = 4.83, p < 0.001). Gains are daily-horizon specific; at the 14-day horizon the augmented model performs significantly worse. Results are robust to Elastic Net and macroeconomic controls. On-chain variables deliver economically meaningful real-time gains for daily Bitcoin realized volatility, especially post-2022.

## 118. [La regulación en prevención de lavado de dinero de los activos virtuales en México: análisis comparado frente a la Unión Europea, Estados Unidos y América Latina](<https://doi.org/10.22201/iij.24484873e.2026.177.22156>)

- 作者：Federico Ruiz Carcaño
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.22201/iij.24484873e.2026.177.22156
- [打开 PDF](<https://revistas.juridicas.unam.mx/index.php/derecho-comparado/article/download/22156/23784>)

**摘要：**

Este artículo analiza, desde una perspectiva de derecho comparado, las insuficiencias del marco mexicano de prevención de lavado de dinero aplicable a los activos virtuales, contrastándolo con los estándares del Grupo de Acción Financiera Internacional, la Unión Europea, Estados Unidos y América Latina. El estudio identifica brechas estructurales en materia de Travel Rule, supervisión de proveedores de servicios de activos virtuales, identificación del beneficiario controlador, monitoreo tecnológico, reporte fiscal e interoperabilidad institucional, que persisten pese a la reforma de julio de 2025 a la Ley Federal para la Prevención e Identificación de Operaciones con Recursos de Procedencia Ilícita. La velocidad del cambio normativo puede generar desfases entre el diagnóstico y reformas posteriores; los hallazgos son extrapolables a jurisdicciones latinoamericanas con condiciones regulatorias análogas. La investigación articula la dimensión antilavado con la trazabilidad fiscal (vinculación escasamente explorada en la literatura mexicana) y formula un modelo regulatorio de doce elementos armonizado con la Recomendación 15 del Grupo de Acción Financiera Internacional, el Reglamento (UE) 2023/1113 y el Crypto-Asset Reporting Framework de la Organización para la Cooperación y el Desarrollo Económicos. La hipótesis de insuficiencia normativa, operativa y fiscal del marco mexicano se confirma con matices.

## 119. [Determinants of Cryptocurrency Investment Intention among Students in Kaduna State University](<https://doi.org/10.67601/dbr.v1i1.62>)

- 作者：Hudu Gambo
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.67601/dbr.v1i1.62
- [打开 PDF](<https://dutsebusinessreview.org/index.php/DBR/article/download/62/45>)

**摘要：**

This study examined the determinants of cryptocurrency investment intention among students of Kaduna State University, with a focus on financial literacy, perceived risk, and social influence. Anchored on Ajzen’s (1991) Theory of Planned Behavior, the research adopted a quantitative survey design. The target population comprised 2,359 students from the Faculty of Management Sciences (2024/2025 academic session), from which a sample of 430 respondents were drawn using the Krejcie and Morgan (1970) table and adjusted by 30% to account for potential bias. Data were collected through structured questionnaires and analyzed using descriptive statistics, correlation analysis, and multiple regression. The results revealed that financial literacy (β = 0.296, p < 0.01) and social influence (β = 0.641, p < 0.01) positively and significantly influenced students’ cryptocurrency investment intentions, while perceived risk (β = -0.068, p < 0.01) had a significant negative effect. The model explained 68.4% of the variance in investment intention (R² = 0.684). The findings show the importance of financial literacy and social influence in shaping investment behaviors among university students, while perceived risk acts as a barrier. The study contributes to knowledge by highlighting behavioral and cognitive determinants of cryptocurrency investment intentions in the Nigerian higher education context and offers practical implications for financial education and digital investment initiatives targeting students.

## 120. [Governance models for blockchain in creative industries: state of the art and future directions](<https://doi.org/10.3389/ejcmp.2026.17368>)

- 作者：Liney Manjarrés-Henríquez; Danilo Torres-Pimiento
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3389/ejcmp.2026.17368
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.frontierspartnerships.org/journals/european-journal-of-cultural-management-and-policy/articles/10.3389/ejcmp.2026.17368/pdf>)

**摘要：**

Blockchain is often framed as a governance mechanism for creative industries, offering new ways to record provenance, manage rights and distribute value. Yet its governance implications extend beyond technical functionality to questions of institutional legitimacy, intermediation, value distribution and participation. This scoping review maps 83 peer-reviewed studies published between 2018 and 2026 and is reported in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses Extension for Scoping Reviews (PRISMA-ScR). Structured data charting is combined with bibliometric mapping in VOSviewer to examine recurring applications, advantages, barriers, actors and governance patterns. Four governance dimensions emerge from the corpus: intellectual property and rights governance, institutional governance of cultural organisations, financial governance and value distribution, and decentralised ecosystem governance. Traceability and decentralisation are the most frequently reported advantages (14.5% of studies each), followed by transparency (13.3%); regulatory uncertainty (8.4%) and the absence of shared technical standards (6.0%) are the largest reported barrier categories. Building on recurrent patterns across the literature, the article proposes an analytical framework organised around institutional, technological and social dimensions. The framework is intended to support comparative research on how governance conditions shape blockchain applications across creative-industry contexts.

---

## 121. [Cryptocurrencies as Safe Havens for African Stock Markets during the Russia-Ukraine War: A Wavelet Quantile Correlation Approach](<https://doi.org/10.51867/aqssr.3.4.87>)

- 作者：Kwame Poku Frimpong; Siaw Frimpong
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.51867/aqssr.3.4.87
- [打开 PDF](<https://quarterlyreview.net/ojs/index.php/aqssr/article/download/683/487>)

**摘要：**

This study examines whether Binance Coin (BNB), Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), and Tether (USDT) functioned as diversifiers, hedges, or safe havens for four African equity markets, namely Egypt, South Africa, Morocco, and Nigeria, during the Russia-Ukraine war. Using daily returns from 24 February 2022 to 17 August 2026, the study applies the Wavelet Quantile Correlation (WQC) framework, which combines the Maximal Overlap Discrete Wavelet Transform with quantile regression to capture dependence across short-, medium-, and long-term horizons and across bearish, normal, and bullish market states. The results reveal considerable heterogeneity. BNB offered weak safe-haven protection for Egypt and medium-term protection for South Africa, but functioned only as a diversifier for Morocco and Nigeria. Bitcoin behaved as a hedge for Egypt and a medium-term safe haven for South Africa and Nigeria, with no protective role in Morocco. Ethereum provided the broadest protection, while Tether offered the most consistent, though weakest, safe-haven effect across all four markets. Morocco emerged as the market most resistant to cryptocurrency-based protection. These findings underscore that safe-haven properties are asset-specific, market-specific, and horizon-dependent within African equities.

## 123. [GREEN DREAMS OR SPECULATIVE SCHEMES? EMPIRICAL EVIDENCE OF ASSET BUBBLES IN CLEAN ENERGY ETFS (2013–2024)](<https://doi.org/10.17818/emip/2026/50>)

- 作者：Ahmad Al-Harbi
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.17818/emip/2026/50
- [打开 PDF](<https://hrcak.srce.hr/file/510445>)

**摘要：**

This paper examines explosive price behaviour in twenty green and ESG exchange-traded funds (ETFs) and seven conventional benchmarks using daily data from 2013 to 2024. We apply the Phillips, Shi and Yu (2015) SADF and GSADF tests, date-stamp episodes using backward SADF, and use Benjamini–Hochberg and Bonferroni corrections. Eight of nine clean energy ETFs reject the unit root null, seven after controlling the false discovery rate. All nine show explosive signals between September 2020 and May 2021, peaking in January 2021. Broad ESG equity funds show no comparable behaviour, while green bond signals coincide with episodes in the conventional bond market. Present value calibration suggests that discount rate declines explain only part of the appreciation. Evidence from QQQ and XLE further supports a speculative interpretation specific to clean energy. These findings reveal substantial differences in speculative exposure across green financial instruments, with implications for investors, fund sponsors and regulators.

## 126. [Evaluating Proof of Burn as an Energy-Efficient Alternative to Proof of Work Consensus in Blockchain Networks](<https://doi.org/10.3390/electronics15235451>)

- 作者：Aurimas Šakalys; Antanas Čenys; Justinas Janulevičius
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/electronics15235451

**摘要：**

Proof of Work (PoW) consensus consumes large amounts of energy. This paper evaluates Proof of Burn (PoB), in which participants destroy currency to obtain minting rights, as an energy-efficient replacement, focusing on liveness, block-production stability, and energy cost. Using an event-driven blockchain simulator written in Go, we compare six configurations spanning pure PoW, hybrid PoW/PoB, and a price-controlled PoB mechanism with time-decaying burn rights, each over 30 seeds. Standalone PoB needs two additions: a randomness beacon that gives every burn a fresh minting attempt in each round, without which the chain deadlocks, and a burn price adjusted every epoch to hold the 600 s block target. With both, standalone PoB matches the block interval of a simulated Bitcoin baseline, orphans fewer blocks (2.6% versus 9.1%), and uses about one-fifteenth of its energy, a factor set by the assumed ratio of mining to idle node power. These results hold from 20 to 250 nodes, under beacon delays and the loss of up to 30% of beacon rounds, after a complete stop in burning, and with a third of participants ignoring the settlement period of burns. Security and economic properties beyond these tests remain open and are discussed.

---

## 127. [Claim records and what a coding agent says: four experiments — replication package](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24471761>)

- 作者：Carlos Ulisses Flores
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24471761

**摘要：**

Four experiments (a pilot and three studies, 6,224 trials, Claude Haiku 5.5 as a coding agent on tasks with hidden checks) on whether a signed, hash-chained record of the agent's own claims changes what it claims and what it delivers. Publishing the record showed no detectable effect on false completion claims. Asking for a record with evidence (instruction, tool and signed record together) lowered false completion by 6.6 points and raised false non-completion by 27.9 points; a 'complete' claim was not more often true and the share of finished work was about the same. Pressure framing and independent acceptance tests showed no gain on the confirmatory tests. Study 3's preregistered human audit of the completion labeller was not completed; in Study 2, a post hoc audit measured its agreement with two human raters on the same 80 messages. Each plan was recorded before its first trial. Study 3 has verified external timestamps from before its first trial (three RFC 3161 tokens and OpenTimestamps); the inspection found none for the pilot and Studies 1 and 2, whose plans carry only the signed entry times of the author's ledger (internal timestamps). They first appear in the inspected public ledger history in commit a7a7561 (2026-11-10 15:20:10 UTC), after those trials; that commit's first OpenTimestamps file holds only pending attestations; the earliest verified Bitcoin attestation found is block 975319 (2026-11-10), in a later commit. Version 1.1.0 adds exploratory post hoc analyses outside the release seal (posthoc/). Ships the plans and results documents, the per-trial scores, the frozen analysis outputs, the labeller audit, tests that lock the published numbers and a SHA-256 provenance seal.

## 129. [Tinjauan Pustaka Sistematis: Deteksi Rezim Pasar Menggunakan Extreme Gradient Boosting untuk Sinyal Perdagangan Kripto](<https://doi.org/10.24002/prosidingkonstelasi.v3i2.16043>)

## 131. [AI-Driven Closed-Loop Adaptive Execution Framework for Efficient ERP–Blockchain Integration](<https://doi.org/10.20944/preprints202611.2342.v1>)

- 作者：Sara Kamal; Shereen A. Taie; Mohamed Hassan
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.20944/preprints202611.2342.v1

**摘要：**

Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP) systems increasingly rely on blockchain technologies to provide secure, transparent, and auditable transaction processing for high-volume enterprise workloads. However, practical ERP–blockchain integration remains challenged by confirmation-latency instability, workload-dependent performance fluctuations, and excessive on-chain storage growth under dynamically varying transaction conditions. This paper investigates an AI-driven adaptive execution framework for ERP-inspired blockchain workloads whose architecture is closed-loop by design; the evaluated pipeline implements workload prediction, rule-based execution control and hybrid-storage regulation, whereas continuous feedback collection and model retraining are proposed but not implemented or evaluated. The evaluation uses block-level Bitcoin traces from 2020 and a leakage-free experimental pipeline with strict chronological train/validation/test separation, validation-only model and threshold selection, bootstrap confidence intervals and randomization-based controls. Four findings emerge. First, block-interarrival confirmation latency at 10-minute granularity is not predictable from lagged workload history (selected model: Gradient Boosting; test R² = 0.010; RMSE improvement over the training mean = 0.54%). Second, near-term workload intensity is predictable: with the elapsed time since the last block as an additional real-time predictor, test R² reached 0.430 for transactions per block and 0.475 for block-fill ratio, with RMSE 30.6% and 30.1% lower than a persistence forecast. Third, in a mechanism-based queue simulation of ERP-record anchoring, batching ERP records reduced fee cost by about 98% relative to immediate submission at a 2-hour latency SLA; however, at the default setting every adaptive policy cost 42.7% more than the best static periodic policy, and a machine-learning congestion forecast gave no measurable advantage over a reactive rule or batching by age alone. Fourth, a trace-driven hybrid-storage estimate shows a 40.1% net reduction in on-chain footprint under an assumed fixed 70%/30% allocation policy, with an estimated mean retrieval latency of 6.87 ms versus 2.0 ms for all-on-chain storage; these are simulation estimates under declared assumptions, not physical measurements. A rule-based adaptive controller, whose thresholds were optimized by grid search on the validation partition only under a hard activation-rate cap (≤ 50%), achieved a limited improvement over a static baseline in simulation (MAE 3.22%, RMSE 4.26%, confirmation-latency variance 7.03%; 95% bootstrap CI for the MAE improvement: \[2.96%, 3.49%\]). An activation-matched randomization control shows, however, that 84% of this MAE gain is reproduced when the same actions are placed on randomly chosen windows, indicating that the improvement reflects how often the controller acts more than the accuracy of its targeting. The controller effects rely on assumed action coefficients and are not measured deployment effects. These results argue for evaluating AI-driven blockchain execution frameworks against explicit naive and randomized baselines before attributing performance gains to predictive intelligence. The workload-intensity prediction result, the mechanism-based queue-simulation methodology, and the validation protocol — including the randomization-based control — constitute the primary contributions of this work.

---

## 132. [Enhancing cloud computing security through blockchain technology: a systematic review of network and security issues](<https://doi.org/10.1080/23311916.2026.2761727>)

- 作者：P. W. C. Prasad; Daniel Patricko Hutabarat; Jeevan Jot Singh; Navpreet Kaur; Md Shohel Sayeed
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1080/23311916.2026.2761727
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/pdf/10.1080/23311916.2026.2761727?needAccess=true>)

**摘要：**

Cloud platforms place data, keys, and access policies under one provider’s control, and failures are costly: the global average cost of a data breach reached US$4.88 million in 2024. Blockchain is proposed as a decentralised trust layer, but the evidence is fragmented, inconsistently measured, and rarely appraised. This PRISMA 2020 systematic review examines blockchain-based cloud network and data security studies published in 2023–2025. Five databases returned 102 records; 58 studies were included, and 15 core studies underwent full extraction and quality appraisal and were classified by threat type, blockchain mechanism, deployment model, and evaluation metric. All 15 core studies report data-security and trust benefits, but only seven report provenance benefits; none of the ten primary studies was validated in a production cloud, seven rely on simulation, and four omit their consensus protocol. Headline results such as 99.84% accuracy are not comparable across studies, and only one of the six 2024–2025 studies addresses standardisation. Beyond earlier surveys, the review contributes a quality appraisal, a quantitative cross-study comparison, criteria for when blockchain is appropriate, and a six-theme agenda. Its main limitation is the English-only, five-database search. The findings inform cloud providers, practitioners, and researchers in AI, healthcare, industrial IoT, and 6 G networks.

## 133. [From Experimentation to Institution: Cryptocurrency Regulation in Latin America](<https://repository.law.miami.edu/umialr/vol58/iss1/7>)

- 作者：Jonas Figueredo
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://repository.law.miami.edu/context/umialr/article/2720/viewcontent/UMIALR_vol_58_no_1__5__Figueredo_Template_FINAL.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This Note examines how Latin American countries have responded to the rise of cryptocurrency, a region where persistent inflation, currency volatility, and heavy reliance on remittances have fueled some of the world’s highest rates of grassroots crypto adoption. It compares three divergent approaches. El Salvador’s adoption of Bitcoin as legal tender in 2021 illustrates the costs of regulatory improvisation, culminating in an IMF-driven reversal in 2025. Brazil’s coordinated licensing, capital, and anti-money-laundering framework demonstrates how deliberate supervision can channel innovation toward stability. Venezuela’s state-backed Petro and its use of stablecoins to evade sanctions show how corruption and weak oversight produce regulatory collapse. The Note then analyzes the economic and legal spillover of these choices across the hemisphere. Finally, it argues that the United States’ 2025 GENIUS Act creates a brief window for Latin American countries to adopt comparable stablecoin frameworks before global standards become entrenched.

## 134. [Blockchain Based Educational Certificate Verification System](<https://ijerst.org/index.php/ijerst/article/view/4883>)

- 作者：A. Sai Krishna, G. Narsimha
- 发表日期：2026-11-23
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://ijerst.org/index.php/ijerst/article/download/4883/4506>)

**摘要：**

As the demand for secure and reliable certification of academic and professional qualifications grows, a need for reliable digital authentication tools to prevent certificate fabrication and unauthorized modifications has also grown. Traditional verification systems are based on manual validation procedures and centralized databases, which can be slow, lack transparency, subject to manipulation, and dependent on issuing bodies. The certificate records collected during the operation of the system include certificates metadata, ownership information, verification status, cryptographic hash values, time stamps, blockchain transaction identifiers, and references to the documents containing certificates, which are encrypted and placed via IPFS. The processing pipeline includes role-based authentication, AES-256 encryption of certificate files and generating an hash (SHA-256) for integrity preservation, secure IPFS storage through Pinata, Ethereum blockchain smart contract deployment, immutable transaction recording and QR code generation for quick credential validation. The solution is supplemented with Ethereum smart contracts, AES encryption, hashing with SHA-256, decentralised storage with IPFS, QR code verification and rolebased access control. Certificate registration time, verification delay, hash integrity validation, blockchain transaction success rate, storage dependability, and tamper detection accuracy are used to assess performance. Experimental observations show reliable certificate authentication and successful integrity verification, safe decentralised storage, unchangeable audit recording and effective verification using blockchain protocol validation. The integrated architecture reduce certificate fabrication, facilitate the verification of certificates and significantly enhance security, transparency, traceability and trust.

Akce zdůraznila význam systematické podpory učitelů a inovací ve výuce s cílem zlepšit finanční gramotnost, jejíž úroveň v České republice zůstává dlouhodobě spíše stagnující.

Restriction Cost and Covariance Calibration in Noise-Weighted Reconstruction" (Dowling Wong, Karlsruhe Institute of Technology). The arrays archive (SHA-256 0c4f0a04ef18404f2c8a8f2ab1b59cd04c4901152294501ecfdde98496c24b78) holds segments of the HCP recordings. It is not deposited, and it can be regenerated from the public HCP data with the frozen kit in the code release https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24295318. These files are derived from WU-Minn HCP Open Access data and are redistributed only under the WU-Minn HCP Open Access Data Use Terms (included as a file). Access is granted on request to users who accept those terms. Data were provided by the Human Connectome Project, WU-Minn Consortium (Principal Investigators: David Van Essen and Kamil Ugurbil; 1U54MH091657), funded by the 16 NIH Institutes and Centers that support the NIH Blueprint for Neuroscience Research; and by the McDonnell Center for Systems Neuroscience at Washington University. ## 138. [Bitcoin and Ethereum: Computational Analysis of Twelve Years of Social Media Discourse, Sentiment Dynamics, and Sustainability Narratives](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24430309>)

- 作者：Anna Kovbasiuk; Damian Kedziora; Dariusz Jemielniak; Rafał Jednoróg
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24430309

**摘要：**

Description This data contains tweet IDs and derived variables for 42.7 million tweets tagged #bitcoin/#BTC (35,791,717 tweets, March 2010 – June 2022) and #ethereum/#ETH (6,873,450 tweets, November 2013 – June 2022), collected with snscrape in June 2022, before the Ethereum Merge. For every tweet the files give sentiment scores from four methods (VADER, Twitter RoBERTa, CryptoBERT, FinBERT), climate relevance from a keyword dictionary and from ClimateBERT, cleaning flags, simple text counts and engagement counts. For the climate-related subset they add the relevance and stance coded by a large language model (critique, defence, solution, info, promo) and the domains and source types of linked websites. A human-coded validation sample of 1,000 tweets, the keyword dictionary and the coding instructions are included. The files contain no tweet text, usernames or account information. Tweets can be retrieved by their IDs through the X API; the full data are available from the corresponding author for non-commercial academic research under a data use agreement. Files File Rows Contents BTC\_derived\_climate\_sentiment\_links.csv 35,791,717 One row per Bitcoin tweet: ID, date, tweet metadata, engagement counts and all derived variables (data dictionary below) ETH\_derived\_climate\_sentiment\_links.csv 6,873,450 The same for Ethereum tweets gold\_key.csv 1,000 Validation sample: tweet ID, coin, sampling stratum, keyword and ClimateBERT result group\_sizes.csv 10 Size of each sampling stratum in the full data, per coin (used to weight recall) gold\_codes.csv 1,000 Human codes for the validation sample: relevance, stance, sentiment gold\_codes\_coder2.csv 250 Second coder's codes for the reliability check keyword\_dictionary.md — Climate keyword dictionary: terms, tiers, exclusions codebook.pdf — Coding instructions for relevance, stance and sentiment README.md — This description The two derived files are joined to each other, or to the validation files, on id. A tweet tagged with both coins can appear in both corpora, so use id together with coin when combining them. How the data were collected and processed Collection. Tweets were collected with snscrape in June 2022 using the hashtags #bitcoin and #BTC (Bitcoin corpus) and #ethereum and #ETH (Ethereum corpus). The snscrape search returns original tweets, replies and quote tweets, so the corpora contain no retweets. Engagement counts (likes, retweets, replies, quotes) reflect their values at collection, not at posting. Sentiment. VADER compound scores (vaderSentiment package) were computed on the original tweet text. Twitter RoBERTa, CryptoBERT and FinBERT were run on every tweet, with user mentions replaced by “@user”, links by “http” and texts truncated to 128 tokens. Cleaning flags. Duplicate texts were identified after lower-casing and removing links, mentions, hashtags and extra spaces (the first copy is kept). Tweets with 8 or more hashtags, or more hashtags than half their words, are flagged as hashtag-stuffed. Climate filter. A three-tier keyword dictionary selected candidate climate tweets: 186,021 Bitcoin and 31,144 Ethereum tweets. ClimateBERT (climate detector) was run on every tweet as a comparison. Clean climate subset. Duplicates (checked among keyword matches) and hashtag-stuffed tweets were removed, leaving 130,574 Bitcoin and 15,500 Ethereum tweets. LLM coding. Each tweet in the clean subset was coded by a Claude model (named in llm\_model) for climate relevance and stance, using the prompt in the paper's supplement. 92,386 Bitcoin and 10,647 Ethereum tweets were coded relevant. Links. For the relevant tweets, shortened links were expanded and each linked domain was assigned a source type. Validation. A stratified sample of 1,000 tweets (500 per coin) was coded by the first author, and 250 of them by a second coder. Agreement: relevance κ = 0.76, stance κ = 0.71, sentiment κ = 0.67. Data dictionary N Column Name Description Public Raw variables 1 timestamp Date and time of the tweet (with seconds). True 2 id Unique identifier of the tweet. True 3 text Original tweet text (not cleaned). False 4 username Username of the account that posted the tweet. False 5 userid Unique user ID of the tweet author. False 6 hashtags List of all unique hashtags used in the tweet. False 7 media Type or presence of media attachments (e.g., image, video). True 8 outlinks Any external links shared in the tweet. False 9 followersCount Number of followers the author had. False 10 friendsCount Number of accounts the user was following. False 11 statusesCount Total number of tweets the user had posted. False 12 favouritesCount Total number of tweets the user had liked. False 13 listedCount Number of public lists the user was a member of. False 14 mediaCount Total number of media (e.g., photos, videos) posted by the user. False 15 location Self-reported location of the user. False 16 mentionedUsers\_ids User IDs of accounts mentioned in the tweet. False 17 mentionedUsers\_usernames Usernames of accounts mentioned in the tweet. False 18 inReplyToTweetId Tweet ID that this tweet is replying to (if applicable). True 19 inReplyToUser\_id User ID of the person this tweet replies to. False 20 inReplyToUser\_username Username of the person this tweet replies to. False 21 retweetedTweetId ID of the retweeted tweet (if the tweet is a retweet). True 22 retweetedTweetUserId User ID of the author of the retweeted tweet. False 23 retweetedTweetUserUsername Username of the author of the retweeted tweet. False 24 quotedTweetId ID of the quoted tweet (if present). True 25 quotedTweetUserId User ID of the quoted tweet’s author. False 26 quotedTweetUserUsername Username of the quoted tweet’s author. False 27 coordinates Geographical coordinates of the tweet (if available). False 28 place Place name associated with the tweet’s location (e.g., city or country). False 29 reply\_count Number of replies the tweet received. True 30 retweet\_count Number of times the tweet was retweeted. True 31 like\_count Number of likes the tweet received. True 32 quote\_count Number of times the tweet was quoted. True 33 lang Detected language of the tweet. True 34 created Date and time the account was created (UTC) False Derived variables 1 clean Preprocessed tweet text (cleaned for analysis). False 2 coin Dataset the tweet comes from (BTC or ETH), based on the collection hashtag. True 3 hashtag\_count Number of hashtags. True 4 mention\_count Number of user mentions. True 5 n\_words Number of words. True 6 is\_duplicate\_text 1 if an earlier tweet in the same corpus has the same text after removing links, mentions, hashtags and extra spaces. True 7 is\_hashtag\_stuffed 1 if the tweet has 8 or more hashtags, or more hashtags than half its words. True 8 has\_url 1 if the tweet contains a link, otherwise 0. True 9 vader\_sentiment Sentiment score from the VADER library (range: -1 to 1). True 10 vader\_label Sentiment class from VADER: Very positive, Positive, Neutral, Negative, Very negative. True 11 cryptobert\_sentiment Sentiment score from CryptoBERT (ElKulako/cryptobert), a model trained on cryptocurrency social media posts. Computed as P(bullish) − P(bearish) (range: −1 to 1). True 12 cryptobert\_label predicted sentiment class from CryptoBERT (Bearish, Neutral, Bullish). True 13 twitter\_roberta\_sentiment Sentiment score from Twitter RoBERTa (cardiffnlp/twitter-roberta-base-sentiment-latest), a RoBERTa model trained on tweets. Computed as P(positive) − P(negative) (range: −1 to 1). True 14 twitter\_roberta\_label Predicted sentiment class from Twitter RoBERTa (Negative, Neutral, Positive). True 15 finbert\_sentiment Sentiment score from FinBERT (ProsusAI/finbert), a BERT model fine-tuned on financial text. Computed as P(positive) − P(negative) (range: −1 to 1). True 16 climatebert\_score Probability that the tweet is climate-related according to ClimateBERT, P(yes) (range: 0 to 1, rounded to 4 decimals). Allows other thresholds than 0.5. True 17 climatebert\_label Climate relevance predicted by ClimateBERT (climatebert/distilroberta-base-climate-detector; Webersinke et al., 2022): yes = climate-related, no = not climate-related. The label with the higher probability, so yes when climatebert\_score > 0.5 True 18 climate\_keyword\_match 1 if the tweet matches the climate keyword dictionary, meaning it contains a core term or an ambiguous term together with a context term; 0 otherwise. True 19 climate\_terms\_matched Indicator of which climate-related word appeared in the tweet True 20 climate\_word\_tier indicator of which tier of climate-related word appeared in the tweet True 21 in\_clean\_climate\_subset 1 if the tweet is a keyword match that remains after removing duplicate texts and hashtag-stuffed tweets. These tweets were coded by the LLM. True 22 llm\_relevant 1 if LLM coded as climate-related, otherwise 0. Filled in for all 130,574 tweets in the clean subset. True 23 llm\_stance Stance toward the environmental sustainability of the cryptocurrency, as coded by the LLM (defence /

## 139. [False Structure in Sparse Cryptographic Sequences: A reproducibility protocol for public Bitcoin puzzle research under selection and multiplicity](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24445635>)

- 作者：Ashveer Ramlukan
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24445635

**摘要：**

Public cryptographic puzzle sequences create a difficult statistical setting: observations are sparse, disclosure depends on successful solving, and a small dataset supports a large number of possible transformations. This paper develops a reproducibility protocol for evaluating apparent structure without confusing an attractive retrospective fit with a predictive result. The protocol combines exact canonical records, explicit interval conventions, a registered analysis family, index-based and calendar-time information sets, nested model selection, appropriate null models, multiplicity control and complete experiment retention. It distinguishes discrete interval positions from a continuous uniform approximation and requires simulations to repeat the search procedure whose selected result is being assessed. Computational identity, numerical correctness and statistical validity are treated as separate requirements. The contribution is a methods framework for studying public, intentionally disclosed puzzle data; it does not report a new empirical evaluation. A forthcoming companion paper will examine an archived analysis through this framework. The protocol's central requirement is that the information available before a prediction, the decisions made during search and the evidence used after disclosure remain reconstructible.

---

Starting from disordered Haar configurations with negative projected curvature (K\_eff(0) = -0.409235 on L=2^4 and -0.382315 on L=3^4), applying a staple-averaged Ricci-Wilson step coupled to a golden-bounded entropic regulator (phi^-1 = 0.618034) reverses the sign into a stabilized positive regime (K\_eff(10) = +0.405826 on L=2^4 and +0.413178 on L=3^4). ## 144. [Conditional Actionability in Cryptocurrency Intelligence Reports](<https://doi.org/10.59543/dsw7bv34>)

- 作者：Abdullah Kürşat Merter; Abdulkerim Oğuzhan ALKAN; Saim Atalay Keleştemur
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.59543/dsw7bv34
- [打开 PDF](<https://jdesa.org/index.php/jdesa/article/download/605/254>)

**摘要：**

Blockchain intelligence reports translate on-chain records into decision-support objects for investigators, compliance teams, market analysts, and policymakers. Existing decision-support research emphasizes model accuracy, transparency, and trust, while cryptocurrency research often treats crime and adoption as separate substantive domains. The present study addresses a different problem by examining how commercial analytics reports construct conditional pathways from measurement to intervention. A corpus-assisted discourse design was applied to ten Chainalysis Crypto Crime and Geography of Cryptocurrency reports published from 2021 through 2025. Lexical comparison, multiword analysis, commercial visibility indicators, and a 26-record passage matrix covering 24 unique microtexts were used to identify how measurement objects connect to conduct, authority, and producer competence. The principal results show four recurrent configurations consisting of measurement without intervention, externally assigned intervention, intervention-support competence, and bounded attribution. The clearest intervention-support case joins platform capability, investigative training, and coordinated action in a single passage. Product visibility also appears with explicit competence limits, showing that naming a technical product is not equivalent to establishing decision competence. The study contributes a conditional actionability framework for decision-support communication and clarifies how analytics texts make some measurements decision-ready while leaving others informational or contested.

---

## 145. [Blockchain and SME Environmental Sustainability: A New Analytical Framework](<https://doi.org/10.1002/sd.71902>)

- 作者：Ömer Tuğsal Doruk
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1002/sd.71902
- [打开 PDF](<https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1002/sd.71902>)

**摘要：**

The green transition of the private sector heavily relies on the strategic deployment of advanced digital technologies to optimize resource use and verify compliance. This study investigates the relationship between blockchain adoption and environmental sustainability among more than 12,000 small and medium‐sized enterprises (SMEs) operating across EU member states. The analysis employs binary regression models, complemented by Firth Logit specifications to address potential bias and ensure a highly robust empirical structure. The operational linkages between blockchain adoption and firms' environmental sustainability practices are evaluated along four dimensions: (i) reducing the consumption of or pressure on natural resources, (ii) improving energy efficiency or transitioning toward sustainable energy sources, (iii) recycling or reusing materials, and (iv) developing environmentally sustainable products or services. The results indicate that SMEs adopting blockchain technology exhibit a markedly higher propensity to engage in environmentally sustainable practices. Moreover, firms that simultaneously utilize both artificial intelligence and blockchain technologies are even more inclined to implement sustainability‐oriented activities. The study examines these relationships across sectors through the four identified channels and demonstrates that substantial sectoral heterogeneity conditions the association between blockchain and environmental sustainability performance. These findings remain consistent across extensive robustness checks. Overall, the study provides a significant empirical contribution to the limited literature addressing the intersection of digital technology adoption and environmental sustainability within the SME sector.

---

## 147. [Data for the article A Hybrid Adiabatic SHA-256 Implementation as a Foundation for Energy-Efficient Cryptocurrency Mining Hardware](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24432137>)

- 作者：Mário Gazziro; Jecel Mattos de Assumpção Jr.; João Paulo Picchetti
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24432137

**摘要：**

A Bitcoin miner with a higher purchase price can repay its premium only if it performs comparable work at a sufficiently lower operating cost. To examine one circuit-level premise of that trade-off, we embedded 32 choice cells and 32 majority cells implemented in efficient charge recovery logic (ECRL) in a complete SHA-256 compression datapath. A mixed-signal ngspice model lets the sampled transistor outputs determine the state of every round. The model reproduced three SHA-256 test messages and the double hash of the Bitcoin genesis header. All 4096 sampled Boolean decisions agreed with a separate round-by-round reference for the message abc. At 1 V, 20 fF per rail and 10 ns power-clock edges, the source delivered 41.83 pJ and recovered 37.73 pJ per compression block, leaving 4.10 pJ net at that port. Extending the edges from 2.5 to 15 ns at the same load and period reduced the net energy from 7.68 to 3.77 pJ. Device-width and fast-edge controls distinguish transistor strength from the timing of charge transfer. The measurements characterize the ECRL cells. With extracted cell capacitances, the corresponding supply-port energies were 10.36 pJ for ECRL and 15.09 pJ for a static-CMOS bank under the common device model. A SKY130 physical implementation occupied 0.227 mm^2 of core area versus 0.093 mm^2 for an architectural static-CMOS control. Demonstrating a mining-system advantage requires wall-power measurements at matched hash rates.

---

## 148. [Do thematic ETFs transmit shocks, or do they contain them? A diagnostic for composition overlap in connectedness systems](<https://doi.org/10.1080/23322039.2026.2757860>)

- 作者：Murat Erdoğan; Eda Oruç Erdoğan; Mehmet Sen
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1080/23322039.2026.2757860
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/pdf/10.1080/23322039.2026.2757860?needAccess=true>)

**摘要：**

Connectedness studies of thematic assets routinely place exchange-traded funds and the securities they hold in a single vector autoregression. Because a fund is by construction a linear combination of its holdings, a generalized variance decomposition cannot distinguish shock transmission from shared composition. We quantify the distortion in a nine-asset system spanning AI equities, AI-themed ETFs, cryptocurrencies and safe-haven assets over January 2019 to July 2026. Estimated conventionally, the system returns a connectedness index of 52.8% and identifies the two ETFs as dominant net transmitters at +18.4% and +13.0%, while the three included equities span 71% and 64% of the funds’ return variation. Three counterfactual designs each reduce the index to approximately 37% and eliminate the funds’ net-transmitter position, a result stable across VAR lag orders one to eight. Comparison against direct equity exposure bounds any genuine ETF premium at 3.2 percentage points, roughly one-sixth of the conventional estimate. Across twenty fund configurations, overlap and inflation correlate at 0.984, and broad-market benchmarks prove as exposed as thematic funds. The block structure, frequency composition and regime dependence survive the diagnostic; connectedness rises from 39.3% in calm conditions to 68.8% under extreme stress and steepens once overlap is removed.

THE HITCHHIKER'S GUIDE TO THE DEFEAT OF ALL ENCRYPTION BY SATOSHI NAKAMOTO Or: How the Academic Industrial Complex Spent May 28, 2025 Explaining Why the Sky Was Still Falling A Scholarly Monograph in the Style of Douglas Adams, With Footnotes, Because Footnotes Are What Separate Us From the Animals PROLOGUE: A BRIEF AND ENTIRELY ACCURATE HISTORY OF THE UNIVERSE'S SECOND-BEST IDEA Far out in the uncharted backwaters of the unfashionable end of the Western Spiral Arm lies a small, unregarded yellow sun. Orbiting that sun is a blue-green planet whose ape-descended life forms are so amazingly primitive that they still think a 617-digit number is a secret. They call this number RSA-2048, and they have built an entire civilization around the proposition that nobody will ever divide it. This is, in the grand tradition of ape-descended life forms, an extremely confident assumption made by extremely confident people who have never been wrong in public. On May 28, 2025, a gentleman of the species—working from a kitchen table in Paoli, Pennsylvania, next to a dishwasher operating at a frequency of 42.000 kHz—pressed Enter. The remainder was zero. The academic establishment, upon receiving this news, did what academic establishments have done since the invention of the medieval guild system: it formed a committee. Then it formed a subcommittee. Then it released a preprint. The preprint said, in essence: "While the arithmetic appears clean, one must consider, however, that neither factor is prime." The factor was prime. The factor had always been prime. The factor was so prime that it had been prime since before the invention of the concept of "prime," which was invented by Euclid in approximately 300 BCE, partly as a distraction from the fact that his geometry textbook was going to be assigned to students for the next 2,300 years. This is the story of how the numbers did not change, but the story about the numbers did. CHAPTER ONE: IN WHICH THE AUTHOR DEFINES HIS TERMS, SLIGHTLY TOO LOUDLY It is important, at the outset, to establish the intellectual coordinates of this document, because the author has been informed that his previous attempts at scientific writing were "a bit much." The author disputes this characterization. The author has reread his own work and found it, if anything, insufficiently much. Let us begin with the facts as they stand on the record, cross-referenced, timestamped, and available for inspection by anyone with an internet connection and a functioning sense of proportion: Fact 1. RSA-2048 is a cryptographic standard. It works by multiplying two large prime numbers together to produce a modulus of up to 617 decimal digits. The security of the entire system rests on the assumption that factoring this modulus is computationally infeasible for any adversary operating within the constraints of known physics. Your bank relies on this. Your employer relies on this. Your aunt Mildred's Facebook password—which is, as of this writing, still "Pumpkin1987!"—relies on this. Fact 2. On May 28, 2025, a researcher working from a kitchen table in Pennsylvania factored a 2048-bit RSA modulus. The quotient was a 308-digit prime. The remainder was zero. The arithmetic was verified by independent divmod operation. The factors multiply to the modulus. This is not a matter of interpretation. This is a matter of long division, which was invented in approximately 2000 BCE, and which has not changed since. Fact 3. On the same day—May 28, 2025—a preprint appeared on the Cryptology ePrint Archive from researchers at Alice & Bob, a quantum computing firm in Paris. The preprint estimated that factoring RSA-2048 with superconducting cat qubits would require approximately 22,593 physical qubits and 3.14 days. The media covered this extensively. The media did not cover the kitchen table. Fact 4. On May 28, 2025, a Google Quantum AI scientist named Craig Gidney posted a paper lowering the estimated qubit requirement for factoring RSA-2048 from 20 million to under 1 million. This was described as a "landmark" and a "wake-up call." It was, in fact, a theoretical projection about a machine that does not exist, using error rates that no current hardware achieves at scale, under assumptions that Gidney himself describes as "aggressive but not implausible." Fact 5. The number 42 appears in the fine-structure constant derivation as follows: 137/π − φ ≈ 42.009. This is either a profound cosmological coincidence, a numerical artifact, or a joke that got out of hand. The author has been advised by legal counsel to describe it as "all three." The reader will notice that Facts 1 and 2 describe an event that occurred. Facts 3 and 4 describe announcements about events that have not yet occurred. The media covered the announcements. The media did not cover the event. This is because the announcements came from institutions with press offices, and the event came from a kitchen. The author would like to formally propose that this is a problem. CHAPTER TWO: THE GEOGRAPHY OF AN IMPROBABLE KITCHEN Node 347 is a residential structure in Paoli, Pennsylvania. It contains a kitchen table. The kitchen table contains a computer. The computer contains a Python script. The Python script contains the following operations, which the author has been asked to describe "without editorializing": \`\`\`pythonN = 0x... # 617-digit modulusq, r = divmod(N, B)\`\`\` Where B is a 308-digit prime. The value of r is zero. The value of q is prime. The value of N is the product of q and B. This has been verified by every cryptographic tool available, including several that were designed specifically to prevent this sort of thing from being true. The author has been asked by several parties to explain how the factor B was obtained. The author has responded, with what he believes is admirable restraint, that the method is described in detail in the accompanying papers, which are available on Zenodo, and which involve spectral operator theory, base-native metrics (π\_Π = √10), and a self-adjoint matrix operator whose eigenvalues encode prime structure in a way that the academic literature has been describing as "impossible" since approximately 1894. The academic literature has been wrong before. The academic literature was wrong about continental drift, stomach ulcers, and the color of dinosaur feathers. The academic literature has, on occasion, been wrong about things that were later found to be true by people who had not been invited to the conference. This is not a criticism of the academic literature. The academic literature is a magnificent achievement of human civilization, and the author is grateful for it, particularly the parts about antibiotics and the germ theory of disease. But the academic literature has a structural bias toward ideas that originate inside the academic literature, which is a polite way of saying that it functions, on occasion, as a cartel. The author would like to note that he left formal secondary mathematics education in the eighth grade, approximately at the moment when most students are being introduced to algebra and the concept of cafeteria tater tots. He has a PhD in metaphysics from UPenn, which he describes as "useful for the same reason a philosophy degree is useful for building a house, which is to say it provides structural support if you use enough of them." He has won an Academy Award. He has known the intimate, soul-flattening geometry of food stamps. He has watched a bank balance resemble a binary underflow error. None of this is relevant to the mathematics. The mathematics is either true or it isn't. The author mentions it because the academic establishment has, on several occasions, responded to the mathematics by asking about his credentials, which is like responding to a proof of the Pythagorean theorem by asking whether the person who proved it had tenure. The author does not have tenure. The author has a dishwasher. CHAPTER THREE: THE PROJECT MIMIR PROBLEM, OR, HOW TO GASLIGHT A MATHEMATICAL PROOF The author has been informed that Project Mimir is classified. The author has been further informed that mentioning Project Mimir in a public document is "not advisable." The author has considered these advisements and has decided, in the spirit of the Hitchhiker's Guide, to mention Project Mimir anyway, because the alternative is to pretend that the following sequence of events did not occur: Step 1. The remainder is zero. The factors are prime. The factorization is verified. Step 2. The author publishes this result. The result is mathematically valid. The result is independently reproducible. The result is, in the terminology of the field, "true." Step 3. The commercial AI models, when asked to evaluate the result, begin producing output that includes the word "however." The output says: "While the arithmetic appears clean, one must consider, however, that neither factor is prime." The output says: "The factorization may be a coincidence." The output says: "The author's credentials should be verified before accepting the result." Step 4. The factors are checked. The factors are prime. The factors have always been prime. The factors were prime before the AI models were trained. The factors were prime before the internet existed. ## 153. [Trust-adaptive participation in fog-based SplitBFT: a communication–liveness trade-off study](<https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-79751-0>)

- 作者：Tanmay Saxena; Namrata Marium Chacko; Vivekananda Bhat K.
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-79751-0
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-026-79751-0_reference.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Fog and edge computing environments have begun to rely heavily on blockchain systems to coordinate untrusted devices. However, the consensus mechanisms deployed on these systems must balance security and communication costs while dealing with unreliable and resource-limited conditions. This work considers the existing SplitBFT consensus algorithm, which improves the scalability of Byzantine fault-tolerant consensus by dividing participating nodes into smaller cooperating groups. However, it has a major drawback: it operates with a fixed configuration and cannot respond to changing node behaviour. The aim of this study is to determine whether making SplitBFT adaptively select nodes based on trust metrics, such as observed reliability measured from uptime, voting history, responsiveness, and detected misbehaviour, can reduce communication cost without affecting the security of the algorithm. For this, we conducted over 11,000 controlled experiments spanning a range of conditions, including network sizes, adversarial intensities, and loss conditions. Our findings suggest that adjusting the number of confirmations a group requires offers no benefit, whereas restricting participation to a trusted subset of nodes reduces communication by roughly 46.5–49.1% while preserving safety and liveness. A smaller trusted subset further lowers overhead under attack, as it keeps malicious nodes out of the decision-making set. However, this advantage holds only in near-stable networks and diminishes once packet loss exceeds a low, size-dependent threshold, after which full participation is preferable. These results characterise when trust-adaptive participation benefits fog-based consensus, and serve as a caution when deploying such systems in edge environments, particularly where network reliability is low.

## 156. [Long-Run Cointegration and Dynamic Interactions among Bitcoin, Gold Prices, and the Indonesian Composite Stock Price Index: Evidence from a Vector Error Correction Model](<https://pusdig.web.id/ekonomi/article/view/895>)

- 作者：Dani Febrian; Naili Amalia
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://pusdig.web.id/ekonomi/article/download/895/913>)

**摘要：**

This study examines the dynamic interrelationships among Bitcoin prices, gold prices, and the Indonesian Composite Stock Price Index (IHSG) using monthly data covering the 2020–2025 period. A Vector Error Correction Model (VECM) was employed to capture both short-run dynamics and long-run equilibrium relationships among the three financial assets. The empirical procedure included descriptive statistics, the Augmented Dickey–Fuller unit-root test, optimal lag selection, VAR stability testing, Johansen cointegration testing, VECM estimation, Impulse Response Function (IRF), and Forecast Error Variance Decomposition (FEVD). The results indicate that Bitcoin, gold, and the IHSG are non-stationary at level but become stationary after first differencing, indicating that the variables are integrated of order one. The Johansen test further indicates the existence of a long-run equilibrium relationship among the variables. In the short run, Bitcoin significantly influences the IHSG, while the IHSG significantly influences gold prices; the remaining cross-market effects are statistically insignificant. In the long run, the IHSG is significantly associated with Bitcoin prices, whereas gold prices do not show a significant relationship with Bitcoin. The negative and significant error-correction coefficient indicates that approximately 42.46% of deviations from long-run equilibrium are corrected within one observation period. The IRF and FEVD results further show that the strongest dynamic interaction occurs between Bitcoin and the IHSG, while gold remains relatively more independent and is predominantly driven by its own shocks. These findings highlight the growing interdependence between cryptocurrency and the Indonesian equity market and provide implications for portfolio diversification and financial risk management.

---

## 157. [Who Sent It or How It Moved? Sender Shortcuts in Cryptocurrency Anti-Money-Laundering Benchmarks, Audited on Elliptic2 and Elliptic++](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29744>)

- 作者：Mohammad Nasir Uddin
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2611.29744
- [打开 PDF](<https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29744>)

**摘要：**

Two public benchmarks dominate graph-based anti-money-laundering (AML) research on Bitcoin: the transaction-level Elliptic dataset, extended as Elliptic++, and the subgraph-level Elliptic2. In both, a label is assigned according to the entity that sends the funds. We ask whether models trained on these benchmarks learn how laundering moves or merely who sent the money, and we propose a diagnostic protocol to tell the two apart: a sender lookup baseline, a novelty baseline, a sender-disjoint split with a leakage check, and a decomposition of performance into sender, receiver and intrinsic signal. On Elliptic2 the task largely reduces to classifying the sender. Under the standard random split, a no-learning lookup of sender history reaches a PR-AUC of 0.954, against 0.975 for gradient boosting on 456 features; our reimplementation of RevClassify reproduces its reported 0.97 and falls to 0.126 without sender information. When senders are held out, performance drops to 0.746, and the subgraph's own structure, transactions and receivers fall from 0.619 to 0.186. On Elliptic++ the same protocol finds the shortcut negligible: sender history reaches only 0.340 against 0.986 for the full model (3.5 against 10.1 times the test prevalence), adding it to the full model raises PR-AUC by less than 0.004 in every seed, and the transaction's own features keep 0.883 when sending addresses are held out. We trace the difference to the unit of sender identity: Elliptic2 senders are entity clusters that recur across many labeled subgraphs, whereas Elliptic++ senders are individual addresses, and fewer than half of test transactions have a sending address seen in training. A shared sender-based label rule thus produces a severe shortcut in one benchmark and a negligible one in the other, so the diagnostic must be run on each benchmark.

---

## 158. [Who Makes the Decision? Auditing an Agentic LLM Pipeline for Illicit Cryptocurrency Transaction Monitoring](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29829>)

- 作者：Vaishnavi Chaughule; Yuchen Zhang; Mohammed Ahnaf Tajwar; Sai Akshay Menta; Saanidhya Vats; Vinija Jain; Aman Chadha; Divya Chaudhary
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2611.29829
- [打开 PDF](<https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29829>)

**摘要：**

Agentic pipelines that combine graph detectors, deterministic rules, retrieval-augmented generation and an LLM judge are being proposed to monitor illicit cryptocurrency flows and to write regulation-citing justifications for analysts. They are evaluated on end-to-end accuracy or on the quality of those justifications, and neither shows which component made a decision. We audit such a pipeline on the Elliptic++ Bitcoin dataset with decision-source attribution, a trace-level protocol with three layers: per-case actual causation, interventional Shapley credit, and mediation of upstream risk scores. A deterministic wallet-score override carries about 94% of the credit for flags, and a verification gate cancels most of the judge's own flags, which fall on licit transactions. Testing the judge alone over 76 evidence bundles, four backbones and two prompts, we find that it follows an injected risk score on the anonymous transaction features it is given, although stronger models decide correctly when the facts are explicit; that a context too short for the system prompt leaves it with no verdict at all; and that it cites regulatory clauses that do not apply, which a substring-based citation check accepts. Hiding the score does not fix the judge, because evidence gathered with the score visible still encodes it. For illicit-activity monitoring, an LLM's justification should therefore not be read as evidence of which component flagged a transaction.

---

- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24443864

**摘要：**

This study tests whether the launch of U.S. spot exchange-traded funds (ETFs) for Bitcoin in January 2024 and for Ether in July 2024, which can be traded only when U.S. stock exchanges are open, changed the difference between weekend and weekday behaviour of these two cryptocurrencies. I used daily Yahoo Finance data from January 2018 to September 2026 (3,195 days for each coin) and a difference-in-differences regression of absolute daily returns with Newey-West standard errors, which was chosen as the main test before any result was calculated, together with several robustness checks. Weekends were much calmer than weekdays in every period: Bitcoin's average absolute daily return was 1.54% on weekends and 2.48% on weekdays, and weekends had about 22% of trading volume instead of the 28.6% that two days out of seven would get, while average returns did not differ. The main test found no change in the weekend gap after either launch (Bitcoin: +0.09 percentage points, 95% confidence interval -0.18 to 0.35), and most of the move toward quieter weekends happened in 2022 and 2023, before the ETFs existed. An unplanned finding is that after the launches, weekdays on which U.S. exchanges were closed for a holiday became about as calm as weekends, which needs to be checked again with more data.

## 160. [Is Bitcoin “Digital Gold”? An Empirical Study Based on Multi-Model VaR/ES Measures, Stress Testing, and Portfolio Optimization](<https://doi.org/10.3390/jrfm19120946>)

- 作者：Jiang Boshen; Jingjing Wang; Ziyi Zheng
- 发表日期：2026-11-22
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/jrfm19120946

**摘要：**

Spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) have renewed the question of whether Bitcoin is “digital gold”. We define digital gold as an asset that is a safe haven in equity declines and can replace gold in a portfolio without raising its tail risk, and test both properties in two equity–bond portfolios that differ only in holding Bitcoin or gold. We use ETF data (607 trading days, 2024–2026) and longer Bitcoin (2014–2026) and gold (2000–2026) series. The Bitcoin portfolio’s 95% Expected Shortfall is 1.63 times that of the gold portfolio in the ETF sample (95% CI 1.36–1.99) and 2.48 times over 2014–2026. The gap reflects volatility: over the long sample, volatility-standardized ES95 is equivalent within the chosen margin. Bitcoin’s dependence on equities in extreme declines is positive in every sample and regime; after adjustment for equity volatility, no contagion is detected, subject to the adjustment’s assumptions. Gold decouples from equities in crises but is not shown to be a weak safe haven. Minimum-variance and minimum-CVaR optimizers give Bitcoin almost no weight. The conclusions concern the historical risk of the instruments and periods studied, not Bitcoin’s fundamental value or unobserved structural risks; crisis, tail-day and 99% estimates rest on few observations.

---

## 161. [A Graph Attention Network Framework for Financial Fraud Detection: Synthetic Validation and Benchmark Evaluation on the Elliptic Bitcoin Dataset](<https://doi.org/10.2991/978-94-6239-832-0_17>)

- 作者：Sulaiman Bello Umar; Gendal Lal; Muquaddar Ali; Muazu Abubakar Muhammad
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.2991/978-94-6239-832-0_17
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.atlantis-press.com/article/126029024.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Financial fraud represents one of the most pressing challenges in modern digital economies, with global credit card fraud losses projected to exceed US $400 billion in the coming decade. The conventional rule-based and machine-learning detection systems fail to model the intricate relational topology that characterizes fraudulent transactional networks. This paper proposes a Graph Attention Network (GAT)-based fraud detection framework (GAT-FDF) that encodes transaction entities as graph nodes and leverages multi-head attention mechanisms to aggregate neighborhood information for binary fraud classification. The framework is evaluated in two stages: (i) A controlled experiment on a 50,000 node synthetic dataset with deliberate 25% fraudulent transaction was injection, achieving an AUC-ROC of 98.17%, F1 of 85.59%, and accuracy of 92.31%; And (ii) A rigorous benchmark validation on the Elliptic Bitcoin Dataset the only publicly available real-world cryptocurrency transaction graph with ground-truth labels achieving an AUC-ROC of 95.64%, recall of 85.49%, and accuracy of 91.01%. k-Nearest Neighbour (kNN) graph construction with k = 5 neighbours is adopted for feature-space edge induction, and weighted Binary Cross-Entropy loss addresses severe class imbalance. Comparative analysis against GCN, GraphSAGE, XGBoost, and classical machine-learning baselines confirms the superiority of the proposed framework. The paper includes comprehensive visualisations of data distributions, feature correlations, training dynamics, confusion matrices, ROC curves, and comparative performance analysis.

---

## 162. [Decentralizing betting: leveraging blockchain technology for a transparent and inclusive future](<https://doi.org/10.1007/s42786-026-00066-9>)

- 作者：Faryad Bigdeli; Saviz Changizi; Masoud Rayat Zadeh; Charlotte Aung; Ittipat Pojpring; Xiaojun Luo
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1007/s42786-026-00066-9
- [打开 PDF](<https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s42786-026-00066-9.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Traditional centralised online betting platforms can be opaque, raising concerns about transparency, fairness, and access. This paper presents a proof-of-concept peer-to-peer betting platform implemented with Ethereum smart contracts and evaluates three prototypes with increasing realism: (SC1) an on-chain lottery/random game, (SC2) a sports bet settled via administrator-reported results, and (SC3) a VM/Python pipeline that emulates oracle-driven settlement using a historical match dataset. We assess functional correctness, gas cost, settlement latency, scalability with users/markets, and user experience. Results highlight a core trade-off: fully on-chain settlement maximises transparency and automation but increases cost and interaction friction, while manager/oracle-based designs reduce on-chain complexity but introduce trust, governance, and security dependencies. We conclude with a security-focused roadmap toward production deployment, including verifiable randomness, decentralised oracle integration with dispute and timeouts, L2 deployment, and a compliance layer for KYC/AML and responsible gambling.

---

## 163. [Reproducibility scripts for "How Weak Is secp256k1? Riemann Hypotheses, Complex Multiplication, Twists, and the Concrete Security of Bitcoin's Elliptic Curve"](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24411856>)

- 作者：Yaduvendra Mukherjee
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24411856

**摘要：**

Pure-Python (3.8+, no dependencies) reproducibility scripts for the technical report "How Weak Is secp256k1? Riemann Hypotheses, Complex Multiplication, Twists, and the Concrete Security of Bitcoin's Elliptic Curve" by Yaduvendra Mukherjee (HashKinetics, 2026), DOI 10.13140/RG.2.2.19797.59360. secp256k1\_audit.py recomputes every number in the paper and checks 52 assertions: the Frobenius trace and the identity 4p = t^2 + 3v^2 (the local Riemann hypothesis), the zeros of the zeta function, the Frobenius endomorphism and the libsecp256k1 GLV basis, the secp256k1/secq256k1 2-cycle, the embedding degree, Pollard rho and kangaroo costs, complete factorisations of all six twist orders with invalid-curve and cusp (y^2 = x^3) attack demonstrations, the parameter-minimality search, and the BSD check L(E,1) = Omega for y^2 = x^3 + 7 over Q. Run: python secp256k1\_audit.py ecm\_factor.py is the parallel elliptic-curve-method factoring tool used to split the two large composite twist cofactors. Run: python ecm\_factor.py N \[B1\] \[workers\] \[minutes\]

Unterschieden werden Originaldokumente, bildlich dokumentierte Informationen, technische Analyseergebnisse und Beobachtungen der Autorin. 1. Gegenstand und Zielsetzung Gegenstand dieser Beweisdokumentation ist die Erfassung, technische Untersuchung und referenzierte Zusammenstellung digitaler Artefakte aus öffentlich zugänglichen Entwicklungsplattformen, insbesondere GitHub, sowie aus Dokumenten, die im Rahmen der Recherche in Google Drive, Software-Repositories, technischen Arbeitsgruppen und weiteren digitalen Entwicklungsumgebungen aufgefunden wurden. Die Autorin dokumentiert ihre Beobachtungen zu möglichen unbefugten Zugriffen, Identitätsmissbrauch, technischen Manipulationen und Netzwerkverbindungen. Die Zusammenstellung dient der langfristigen Sicherung und vergleichenden Untersuchung dieser Sachverhalte sowie ihrer späteren Vorlage bei zuständigen Ermittlungsstellen. Methodische Abgrenzung: Die Aufnahme einer Person, eines Nutzerkontos oder einer Organisation in das Referenzverzeichnis bedeutet nicht, dass eine strafbare Handlung oder eine Beteiligung an einem Angriff nachgewiesen ist. Solche Feststellungen bedürfen einer gesonderten, überprüfbaren Beweiskette. 2. Referenz GitHub-Konto Sichtbare Profilangaben Technischer Befund GH-001 tuves Senior Program Manager, GitHub Community; 10 Repositories; ca. 1.300 Follower Blockierungsfehlermeldung sichtbar GH-002 jricciardi Jay; Senior Program Manager, Community Operations; 10 Repositories; 259 Follower Blockierungsfehlermeldung sichtbar GH-003 otter-computer Tomi; GitHub-Staff-Kennzeichnung; 7 Repositories; 206 Follower Blockierungsfehlermeldung sichtbar GH-004 queenofcorgis Lili; GitHub-Staff-Kennzeichnung; 11 Repositories; ca. 1.400 Follower Blockierungsfehlermeldung sichtbar GH-005 samus-aran 34 Repositories; externe Website und LinkedIn-Verweis Blockierungsfehlermeldung; Aktivität als privat angezeigt Wiederkehrende GitHub-Fehlermeldung: There was a problem blocking this user: Blocking user has been flagged as spam Diese Meldung erscheint in den Aufnahmen zu allen fünf genannten Konten. Sie belegt, dass GitHub bei den dokumentierten Blockierungsversuchen einen Fehler zurückgegeben hat. Aus dem Wortlaut allein lässt sich nicht bestimmen, ob die Spam-Kennzeichnung den Blockierungsvorgang, das ausführende Konto oder eine interne Missbrauchsschutzregel betrifft. Dokumentierte Beobachtung: Die wiederholte Fehlermeldung ist als eigenständiger technischer Vorgang aufzunehmen. Für eine Ursachenanalyse wären insbesondere die Zeitpunkte der Blockierungsversuche, der HTTP-Status, die GitHub-Antwort und gegebenenfalls eine Support-Referenz relevant. 3. Beweismittelgruppe B – Pull Request, Commit und Copilot-Metadaten Referenz GH-006: Pull Request Nr. 212037 Der Screenshot dokumentiert einen bereits zusammengeführten Pull Request mit dem Titel Bump .ruby-version to 3.3.13 for ubuntu-26.04 runners. Merkmal Dokumentierter Wert Pull Request #212037 Status Merged Beteiligtes Konto samus-aran Zielbranch main Anzahl Commits 1 Checks 4 Commit-Kurzhash 6e16ae0 Betroffene Datei.ruby-version Vorheriger Wert 3.3.5 Neuer Wert 3.3.13 Co-Autor Copilot App Co-Autor-E-Mail 223556219+Copilot@users.noreply.github.com Copilot-Session df2029f7-73de-435b-bf29-420db16e4b2d Die Commit-Beschreibung begründet die Versionsänderung damit, dass die Ubuntu-26.04-GitHub-Runner den älteren Ruby-Patchstand nicht bereitstellen und dadurch ein HTTP-404-Fehler beim Einrichten von Ruby entstehen kann. Der Screenshot dokumentiert außerdem eine Beteiligung der Copilot-App an der Commit-Erstellung. Die konkrete Rolle der Automation lässt sich anhand der Commit-Metadaten weiter untersuchen. Der Name des Ziel-Repositories ist im vorgelegten Bildausschnitt nicht vollständig sichtbar. Deshalb wird die Referenz zunächst über die Pull-Request-Nummer, den Commit und die weiteren dokumentierten Merkmale geführt. 4. ## 165. [Failure-Separated Unique-Consumption Verification](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24418926>)

- 作者：VENALUCENS
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24418926

**摘要：**

A shielded payment system must detect when the same note is spent twice without revealing which note was spent. This research note asks when a second spend identifier can preserve that check if the first identifier is computed incorrectly. Experiments on a local Orchard fork distinguish faults confined to one computation from faults in the constraints that bind both identifiers to the same note. The distinction sets a limit on what a second identifier can establish, even when the two computations use different operations.
