## 167. [Bitcoin Data Labs L2 Audit Dataset: Liquid Network custody, reserves, peg flows and incidents](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24429492>)

- 作者：Saurabh Kumar
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24429492

**摘要：**

A reproducible audit of Bitcoin layer-2 systems from public chain data alone, starting with the Liquid Network. Every number can be recomputed by anyone with a Bitcoin node and an Elements (Liquid) node; no number comes from the operators. Coverage and versions: this version covers the Liquid Network only. Later versions of this record will add other Bitcoin layer-2 systems (Ark, Spark) to the same common tables. To reproduce a specific number, cite the DOI of the version you used; the record's all-versions DOI always resolves to the latest release. Contents (Parquet, integer satoshis, UTC Unix times): every Bitcoin output that has held Liquid reserves since 2018, and every Bitcoin transaction signed by a federation script (34,635); the reserve against L-BTC outstanding at every Bitcoin block since block 540,000, and per UTC day; every peg-in and peg-out with both legs and the delay between them; peg-out halts and payouts that no peg-out matches; the federation's spending paths, thresholds, keys and timelocks (normal 11-of-15; timelocked 2-of-3 emergency path), key rotations, and the custody the emergency path could spend at each block; Liquid block production and gaps; incidents found by fixed rules (shortfalls, emergency-path spends, block and redemption halts, unexplained outflows), including the September 2026 reserve breach and its restoration. A common audit schema (common/) is designed so that other layer-2 systems fill the same tables; Liquid's own evidence tables are in liquid/. Every row of a common table carries a verification class (bitcoin\_verified, l2\_chain\_verified, cross\_chain\_verified or inferred) and its method. Cutoff: Bitcoin block 976,206 (2026-11-17 02:10:32 UTC) and Liquid block 4,148,532. The release passes 78 invariant checks (metadata/validation.json). Verify the files with sha256sum -c metadata/SHA256SUMS or python3 scripts/verify\_release.py. Limits: bitcoin sent to a peg-in address but never claimed or moved is invisible until the federation moves it, so reserves can be understated, never overstated. The chain shows which addresses paid which; it does not show who controls an address, who authorized a transaction, or why. Method, definitions and known limits: METHODS.md. Release v0.1.1 (tag v2026.11.19) corrects the 2018–2022 emergency exposure of v0.1, which was never published (CHANGELOG.md). Files: l2-data-v2026.11.19.zip holds the complete release (data, metadata, methods, changelog, citation and verification script); README.md and METHODS.md are copies for browsing. Git repository: github.com/bitcoindatalabs/l2-data (this version is tag v2026.11.19).

## 168. [Historical Coefficient Fields: A Controlled Evaluation of Geometric Market-Memory Representations for Financial Forecasting](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24422849>)

- 作者：Said El Qotbi; Rajaa Filali
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24422849

**摘要：**

Code for the manuscript "Historical Coefficient Fields: A Controlled Evaluation of Geometric Market-Memory Representations for Financial Forecasting" (S. El Qotbi and R. Filali, under review). The package computes the Historical Coefficient Field and its eight geometric descriptors, and evaluates five input representations with five learners and AR, HAR and GARCH(1,1) benchmarks on gold (GLD), the S&P 500, EUR/USD and Bitcoin, on identical walk-forward samples. It includes the checks run before any experiment (identities of the coefficient vector, no constant descriptor, causality, folds), the robustness experiments, the Diebold-Mariano tests with Holm correction, the regime and economic analyses, and the scripts that produce every table and figure of the paper. Result files are included. Price data are downloaded from Yahoo Finance on the first run and are not redistributed.

## 169. [BITCOIN BETWEEN TECHNOLOGY, LAW, AND ECONOMICS A Comprehensive Reference for Judiciary and Business in the Age of Digital Assets](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24413697>)

- 作者：mohamed kamal arafa el-rakhawi
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24413697

**摘要：**

This monograph delivers a definitive interdisciplinary examination of Bitcoin across five parts and nineteen chapters, bridging technical architecture, cybersecurity, economic theory, and comparative legal analysis. Part One establishes the foundations of distributed ledgers, cryptographic ownership, consensus mechanisms, and scaling infrastructure. Part Two examines custody architecture, blockchain forensics, and cyber threats. Part Three analyzes monetary policy, the emerging ESG regulatory backlash against proof-of-work energy consumption, corporate treasury applications, and cross-border payments. Part Four provides rigorous legal analysis of Bitcoin's classification as intangible movable property, the fictional situs doctrine resolving jurisdictional conflicts in private international law, the critical distinction between decentralized Bitcoin and sovereign central bank digital currencies, evidentiary standards for blockchain records, judicial attachment challenges, the FATF Travel Rule dilemma regarding non-custodial wallets, and comparative regulatory frameworks across Egypt, the European Union, the United States, and Gulf jurisdictions. Part Five explores advanced dimensions including geopolitical implications, digital inheritance and smart wills, autonomous AI agents transacting in Bitcoin, taxation and Islamic zakat obligations, and the jurisprudential tension between protocol finality and judicial authority. The work integrates practical tools: model contractual clauses for arbitration and settlement, institutional compliance checklists, expanded court-appointed expert witness mandates, a continuous update protocol via Digital Companion, and a multilingual glossary. Supported by landmark precedents including AA v Persons Unknown and comprehensive academic references, this monograph serves as an indispensable reference for judges, lawyers, academics, and business leaders navigating the complex intersection of technology, law, and economics in the digital asset era.

---

## 170. [Temporal Graph Learning for Robust Detection of Coordinated Fraud in Dynamic Transaction Networks](<https://doi.org/10.54097/7nnpma38>)

- 作者：Sofia Lindqvist; Johan Berg; Maya Svensson
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.54097/7nnpma38
- [打开 PDF](<https://ijaetr.org/index.php/ojs/article/download/174/167>)

**摘要：**

Coordinated fraud can produce repeated, synchronized transfers whose signatures are obscured by heterogeneous activity, missing edges, and evolving behavior. This paper studies a lightweight temporal graph learning strategy based on account-level transaction attributes, static neighborhood descriptors, and eight-bin temporal coordination statistics. Static and temporal feature variants are each trained with the same simulated edge-deletion augmentation to disentangle the effects of timing descriptors and robustness training. Experiments are conducted on a controlled, fully specified synthetic benchmark containing 4,800 accounts, approximately 15,000 transfers per run, and simulator-assigned coordinated actors; the benchmark is not a real-world labeled fraud dataset. Three independent runs use chronological, nonoverlapping train/validation/test snapshots. The edge-augmented static graph achieves test average precision 0.631, while the identically augmented temporal feature model reaches 0.602; their nonaugmented counterparts score 0.571 and 0.569, respectively. At 20% and 40% test-edge removal, augmented static-graph average precision is 0.518 and 0.389, respectively. A matched augmentation control, fixed-budget top-100 precision, and timestamp shuffling separate the roles of training robustness and within-snapshot timing. The study provides an efficient, transparent protocol for evaluating coordinated-pattern detection in evolving transaction networks.

## 172. [Revolutionizing Finance: A Critical Analysis of the Regulatory Framework Concerning Digital Lending and Cryptocurrency in India](<https://doi.org/10.36948/ijfmr.2026.v08i06.92590>)

- 作者：Aryaman Srivastava
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.36948/ijfmr.2026.v08i06.92590
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.ijfmr.com/papers/2026/6/92590.pdf>)

**摘要：**

India's digital finance sector is transforming the country's financial landscape. Digital payments and lending, cryptocurrencies, and virtual digital assets are increasingly relevant to this transition. However, persistent regulatory and systemic challenges may hinder their wider adoption. This paper combines theoretical research with empirical analysis to identify these challenges and propose practical solutions.

## 173. [A BLOCKCHAIN-ENHANCED RETRIEVAL-AUGMENTED GENERATION FRAMEWORK FOR DISINFORMATION DETECTION](<https://doi.org/10.26577/jmmcs201211426>)

- 作者：Asem Turarbek; Saltanat Narbayeva; Ahmet Şakir Dokuz; Mukhit Karimov; L. S. Kopbosyn
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.26577/jmmcs201211426
- [打开 PDF](<https://bm.kaznu.kz/index.php/kaznu/article/download/2106/933>)

**摘要：**

Automated assessment of claim veracity remains an open problem in natural language processing, particularly for political statements whose truthfulness is ambiguous, contextual and subject to expert disagreement. This paper presents VeraChain, a hybrid system that combines a fine-tuned DistilBERT classifier, a dual-index retrieval stage (retrieval-augmented classification in the sense of RAG; no text is generated), and a hash-chained ledger that records every verdict together with its evidence and, for borderline predictions, supplies the verdicts of similar past statements as a consensus signal. Both FAISS indices and the ledger are built from the LIAR training split only, and the ledger is frozen for the main test evaluation. A stepwise ablation over five random seeds on the 1,283 LIAR test statements shows that DistilBERT alone reaches 64.36 ±1.25 percent accuracy; adding a single index of REAL statements lowers accuracy by 0.75 percentage points, adding a second index of FAKE statements recovers this loss (+0.87 points, positive in four of five seeds), whereas the probability-ratio score and the ledger consensus step add no consistent gain (−0.05 and −0.11 points). With the original seed, VeraChain reaches 65.24 percent against 64.77 percent for the same system without the consensus step (exact McNemar p = 0.36; 95 percent bootstrap interval of the difference \[−0.39,1.33\] points). Retrieval fusion lowers the expected calibration error of DistilBERT from 0.112 to 0.045, although temperature scaling of DistilBERT alone gives 0.031. On a single laptop GPU, the median processing time is 17.5 milliseconds per statement (95th percentile 20.9 ms), including the write of the ledger entry to disk. The results show that a hash-chained ledger can provide tamper-evident audit records for automated veracity assessment at a small computational cost, whereas its use as a consensus signal does not improve accuracy on LIAR.

---

## 174. [Modeling Joint Tail Risk and Dependence for Cryptocurrencies via a Copula-Based Semiparametric Framework](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28524>)

- 作者：Cathy W. S. Chen; Takaaki Koike; Aaron E. P. Lin
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2611.28524
- [打开 PDF](<https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28524>)

**摘要：**

This study proposes a Bayesian copula-based bivariate semiparametric framework for jointly forecasting Value-at-Risk (VaR) and Expected Shortfall (ES) while modeling cross-asset tail dependence. The marginal dynamics are specified using four ES-CAViaR-type models, including extensions incorporating Rogers-Satchell and Garman-Klass volatility measures. To construct the copula inputs, we use the empirical ranks of standardized residuals, which depend on the marginal parameters, thereby linking marginal tail dynamics and dependence modeling without requiring fully specified marginal distributions. Clayton and BB1 copulas are considered, and adaptive Markov chain Monte Carlo methods are used for estimation and joint tail-risk forecasting. A simulation study under a DCC-GARCH-$t$ data-generating process evaluates the finite-sample forecasting performance of the proposed framework. Using Bitcoin and Ethereum data, we evaluate one-step-ahead forecasts from 2020 to 2025 through a rolling-window forecasting scheme and compare the proposed models with Bayesian DCC-GARCH-N and DCC-GARCH-$t$ benchmarks. Rolling copula estimates also reveal time-varying asymmetry in Bitcoin-Ethereum tail dependence, with lower-tail dependence remaining higher than upper-tail dependence for most of the period. The proposed framework delivers competitive forecasting performance for both marginal and joint tail risk.

---

## 175. [Don't Train It, Write It: An AI Agent Writes New Capabilities into Language Model Weights](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28483>)

- 作者：Santiago Rivadeneira Quintero
- 发表日期：2026-11-21
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2611.28483
- [打开 PDF](<https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28483>)

**摘要：**

We show that an AI agent can write new capabilities into a pretrained language model by adding gated attention heads and MLP neurons inside its existing layers, without backpropagation and without fitting any weight to the answers of task examples. It has a budget of 50 model passes and sees no validation or test instance. Composed in one model, its modules for six BIG-Bench Hard (BBH) subtasks raise the normalized BBH score of Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct from 19.95 to 41.05 (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct: at most 35.01) and that of Gemma-3-1B-it from 10.25 to 32.86. Against the unmodified model at the same precision, both composites leave the next-token distribution on 1,023,000 WikiText tokens unchanged (KL exactly 0), give identical answers on 15,845 items of five other Leaderboard tests and identical scores on the other 18 BBH subtasks. The agent also writes exact multiplication up to 20 digits, running the model only up to 6 (100.0% on the absent lengths of the sealed test in both models; a LoRA with the same budget: at most 2.1%). Sets, rules and report sentences were sealed in git before the first session (manifests timestamped in Bitcoin), with dated amendments listed in Appendix D; a frozen evaluator ran the sealed test once. Verdicts (H5: tracking; H6: composite; H8: one pass vs. chain of thought; H9: fresh and rewritten instances; H10: side effects; H12: multiplication), on the preregistered sealed test, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct: H5 success, H6 success, H8 (web\_of\_lies) success, H9 success, H10 (composite) success on (i) to (iii), (iv) confirmed, H12 success; Gemma-3-1B-it (H7: H5, H6, H8 and H9 in this model): H5 success, H6 success, H8 (web\_of\_lies) success, H8 (tracking 3) success, H9 partial, H10 (composite) success on (i) to (iii), (iv) confirmed, H12 success. Not everything worked: 4 of 45 single modules show small side effects and 5 of 50 sessions hit the 12-hour limit without a module.

## 176. [Whole World United Payment System (WWUPS): A Unified, Interoperable, and Mathematically Modeled Global Financial Infrastructure](<https://doi.org/10.26643/edupub/978-93-92585-06-7>)

- 作者：Mokhdum Azam Mashrafi
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：1
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.26643/edupub/978-93-92585-06-7
- [打开 PDF](<https://drive.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1Xm06D2H0Djm2Zf9edXtkwwR1L5e0BYHJ>)

**摘要：**

Whole World United Payment System (WWUPS) explores the possibility of transforming fragmented national and regional payment systems into a seamless international financial network. The proposed framework focuses on secure, real-time, low-cost and universally accessible financial transactions, supported by a common global interoperability protocol. Inspired by successful interoperable payment innovations such as India's Unified Payments Interface (UPI), the author extends the concept towards a worldwide financial architecture. The book introduces the idea of "One World, One Payment System", emphasizing interoperability, inclusivity, scalability and financial democratization. The publication examines the integration of digital public infrastructure, blockchain-enabled settlements, artificial intelligence-driven fraud prevention, decentralized verification systems and cross-border transaction optimization. It also explores mathematical models, algorithmic frameworks and systems-engineering approaches for transaction routing, liquidity balancing, interoperability governance and cost minimization. By connecting theoretical financial modeling with practical digital payment implementation, this work contributes to discussions on the future of digital currencies, fast payment systems, international financial connectivity and global financial governance.

---

## 177. [The Mechanisms Behind Web3 Meme Success: An Empirical Study of PUPS](<https://doi.org/10.1145/3815189>)

- 作者：Chenhuizi Wang; Chunjing Yu; Li Yang
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：1
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1145/3815189

**摘要：**

This study examines PUPS, a representative Bitcoin ecosystem project, to elucidate the success mechanisms of Web3 meme projects. We test three hypotheses: (H1) community sentiment and social media virality constitute the fundamental drivers of meme asset valuation; (H2) core participants accumulate positions at low prices and distribute at peak valuations; (H3) meme diffusion is predominantly driven by internal imitation, significantly outweighing external marketing effects. Applying event study methodology, social network analysis, and the Bass diffusion model to social media and on-chain data, our findings support all hypotheses, revealing a “propagation–sentiment–trading” pathway. We identify a distinctive “community fingerprint” comprising 348 original holders and 5,036 6-core addresses, characterizing them as both community stabilizers and hype catalysts. This pattern illustrates the paradox of “economic recentralization” within technically decentralized systems. Paradoxically, the founder’s public assertion that “everything will eventually go to zero” evolved into a cultural ritual that reinforced community consensus. This study concludes by proposing a “meme financialization” framework, offering novel perspectives for understanding “Attention as Capital,” “Consensus as Value,” and “Narrative as Asset” in Web3 ecosystems.

---

## 178. [Clustered event2ledger: docker events traceability using hyperledger fabric consortium blockchain](<https://doi.org/10.1186/s13677-026-01023-z>)

- 作者：Marco Antonio Marques; Charles C. Miers; Rafael Rodrigues Obelheiro; Marcos Antonio Simplicio Junior
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：1
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1186/s13677-026-01023-z

**摘要：**

Container virtualization environments provide high flexibility in the way computing resources are used. In particular, the dynamic (de)allocation of containers in response to service loads addresses issues in the scalability and availability of cloud applications. One drawback of such dynamism, though, is that it becomes harder to monitor the constant stream of state changes in the lifecycle of containers. In this article, we tackle this issue by proposing a monitoring solution called Clustered event2ledger, which benefits from consortium blockchain to implement a distributed collection and storage solution that guarantees data integrity, reliability, and availability. Also, it creates a uniform source of information for providers, end-users, and developers of the container-based solution, thus facilitating auditing of resource usage and analyses involving the chronology of performed operations. We also include a security attack model analysis based on identified threats, which shows that Clustered event2ledger follows the best security practices.

---

## 179. [Challenging Bitcoin's dominance: Ethereum's evolving market influence post-Berlin upgrade](<https://doi.org/10.1016/j.frl.2026.111114>)

- 作者：Liron Amor; Koresh Galil; Offer Moshe Shapir
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1016/j.frl.2026.111114

**摘要：**

This study examines whether Ethereum’s market-wide influence strengthened relative to Bitcoin following the Ethereum Berlin upgrade in April 2021. Using one-minute Binance spot data for Bitcoin, Ethereum, and major large-cap cryptocurrencies, we estimate Granger-causality tests and vector autoregressions around the upgrade. The results suggest that Bitcoin retained its benchmark role, but Ethereum’s marginal contribution to short-horizon price discovery increased after Berlin. This shift is especially evident in high- liquidity states, where lagged Ethereum returns became stronger predictors of Bitcoin and other large-cap cryptocurrency returns. The evidence indicates that protocol-level developments may alter the hierarchy of information transmission in crypto markets at the margin, without overturning Bitcoin’s broader benchmark status.

---

## 180. [Airdrop Sybil Attack detection framework supported by machine learning](<https://voltignix.com/journals/index.php/ijets/article/view/26>)

- 作者：Vaisuna; Sambhaji
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://voltignix.com/journals/index.php/ijets/article/download/26/24>)

**摘要：**

A skilled hunter may earn thousands of dollars in tokens from a single airdrop, making airdrop Sybil assaults a profitable endeavor. In this situation, Sybil assaults are not always wanted by projects and are often seen as improper behavior by honest individuals, which may negatively impact a project's image. It is thus to be anticipated that detection methods for Sybil assaults would be continuously enhanced. This study presents a multistep structure. Its concept is to sort blockchain addresses and give each one a score that indicates whether it belongs to the Sybil or regular class. Topologies were classified using a graph isomorphism network, whose parameters were adjusted using a dataset that the authors labeled. In subsequent phases, the account clustering operation was performed using a DBSCAN. Each stage in the framework may be given arbitrary weights by users, which will decide how essential a step is to them and provide a different score for a particular address. A grid search strategy and a threshold after which the address is deemed Sybil were used to find the optimal weights. This article examined a collection of EOAs from the ZK sync rollup. Ultimately, 76% of the examined accounts were classified as Sybils. Compared to the official ZK sync eligibility list, we found 342 addresses that received airdrop tokens but were marked as Sybil by our solution.

---

## 181. [Learning Dietary Effects on Personalized Weight via Cross-Gated Temporal Modeling](<https://doi.org/10.1145/3857354>)

- 作者：Shuying Hong; Donglin Zhang; Weiqing Min; Xiao-Jun Wu
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1145/3857354
- [打开 PDF](<https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3857354?download=true>)

**摘要：**

Weight management plays a crucial role in personal health management. However, most existing approaches rely on calorie-based estimation, which require users to manually record diet and exercise information. This process is inconvenient for daily life and not suitable for long-term use. Considering that taking photos of meals is easier than keeping detailed logs, weight prediction can be performed by effectively using visual and textual dietary data. Therefore, we propose a Cross-feature extraction Time-series model with a Gating mechanism (CTG) that predicts daily weight variations from multi-day dietary and weight data. Specifically, CTG integrates dietary images, textual descriptions, and historical weight records as inputs. CTG employs CLIP to obtain visual-textual representations. We introduce a cross-feature extraction module to model bidirectional dependencies between diet and weight through time-aligned feature selection and linear layer. Besides, we use a GRU-based temporal predictor to accomplish the goal of weight prediction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CTG can achieve promising performance, effectively reducing prediction errors. The code will be available at: https://github.com/pingpighsy/CTG-main.

---

## 182. [Catallactic Statecraft in Tokenised Subsistence Economies: The Structural Failure of Keynesian and Monetarist Stabilisation, Austrian Capital-Structure Mechanics, Dual-Core Finance, and EPI–EVI Boundary Governance](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24378927>)

- 作者：Syed Muntasir Mamun
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24378927

**摘要：**

This companion academic paper bridges the macroeconomic and monetary-theoretical gap left implicit in The Liquid Sovereign (Mamun, 2026o). Across twenty-nine chapters, the volume constructs a formal topology of Flow Sovereignty, the constrained Algorithmic Leviathan, Dual-Core Fractal Statecraft, the Global South Compute Bank (GSCB), and Cognitive and Semantic Sovereignty—and points toward "protocol-driven catallaxies" (Chapter 10)—yet it does not formally derive why Keynesian fiscal stabilisation and Friedmanite monetarism systematically break down in emerging developing economies grounded in tokenised subsistence currencies, nor does it integrate the Economic and Environmental Vulnerability Index (EVI) and Environmental Performance Index (EPI) benchmarking architecture developed in companion KSCCN studies (Mamun, 2026o, 2026d, 2026p). We argue that in developing economies characterised by vast labour informality (informal employment of roughly 88–89% of total employment across Southern Asia and sub-Saharan Africa in ILO estimates, and 84% of the workforce in Bangladesh), narrow fiscal bases (<10% tax-to-GDP), operator-led subsistence e-money wallets, extraterritorial US-dollar stablecoin seepage, and 4.8×–12.3× linguistic AI tokenization premia, Keynesian aggregate demand stimulus tends to invert into near-zero or negative multipliers and fiscal dominance, while Friedmanite monetary rules lose traction through velocity instability, impaired bank pass-through, and digital dollarisation (Mamun, 2026o; LightCastle Partners, 2026; Ilzetzki, Mendoza and Végh, 2011; ILO, 2018; Mishra, Montiel and Spilimbergo, 2010; Aldasoro, Frost and Ito, 2026). Actuating both the wealth frontier (heterogeneous, multi-stage productive capital accumulation) and the income frontier (sustained growth in labour and entrepreneurial marginal productivity) requires a disciplined Austrian–catallactic synthesis: defending a sound unit of account, curtailing Cantillon injection asymmetries, lengthening the Hayekian structure of production through real savings and secure collateralisable property rights, and harnessing decentralised Kirznerian discovery (Sieroń, 2017; Garrison, 2001; Lachmann, 1956; Hayek, 1945; Kerekes and Williamson, 2008). At the same time, contemporary evidence from Argentina's 2023–2026 stabilisation, El Salvador's retreat from its mandatory Bitcoin legal-tender regime, Cambodia's commercialised microfinance debt crisis, and concentrated decentralised finance (DeFi) protocols cautions strongly that pure laissez-faire shock therapy or unregulated crypto-tokenisation is insufficient under deep institutional voids (Sun, Stasinakis and Sermpinis, 2024; World Bank, 2026; Human Rights Watch, 2025). Durable frontier actuation requires embedding Austrian capital-structure and epistemic principles directly inside the protocol architecture of The Liquid Sovereign: Dual-Core Finance (joining concessional Anchor Capital with community-operated Velocity Capital as a category-theoretic pushout), a bifurcated Austrian reserve charter for the Global South Compute Bank (capping fractional compute leverage against correlated outage runs), zero-fee open Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI), and EPI–EVI–HHI boundaries encoded as hard, non-optimisable constitutional projections inside the Reserve of Humanity (Mamun, 2026o; Mamun, 2026p; Mamun, 2026h; Mamun, 2026r; author's computations). From this framework, we derive eight falsifiable predictions (P1–P8) covering the 2026–2035 horizon—calibrated to the 2026–2027 agentic inflection, the November 2029 hard deadline for deferred Least Developed Country (LDC) graduations, and the volume's 2031–2035 systemic bifurcation window (Mamun, 2026o; LightCastle Partners, 2026; United Nations, 2026). Two methodological caveats govern all claims that follow. First, our mathematical verifications of fractional compute-bank leverage, six-node Cantillon sheaf energy, and information-geometric policy updates are illustrative derivations built from stated assumptions and published parameters rather than econometrically calibrated national forecasts (Mamun, 2026o; author's computations). Second, companion KSCCN summaries invoke EVI, EPI, and Herfindahl–Hirschman Index (HHI) market measurement without publishing full standalone algebraic derivations in their public abstracts (Mamun, 2026d, 2026h, 2026e), and both UN EVI and Yale EPI exhibit known weighting and indicator sensitivities (Yale Center for Environmental Law & Policy, 2026a, 2026b); consequently, our formal boundary framework synthesises published UN CDP and 2026 Yale EPI methodologies directly into The Liquid Sovereign's Reserve of Humanity while subjecting every threshold to continuous audit and contestable cohomology (Mamun, 2026o; Yale Center for Environmental Law & Policy, 2026b; UNCTAD, 2026a). KSCCN article. A companion to "The Liquid Sovereign" (10.5281/zenodo.24378053), carrying its Flow Sovereignty, Dual-Core Statecraft, Global South Compute Bank and Reserve of Humanity framework into monetary and macroeconomic theory. Full-colour KSCCN edition with twenty figures (the five figures of the source document, recreated or reproduced, and fifteen added), eleven equations and ten tables. A PDF and an editable DOCX are provided.

---

## 183. [Beyond expectancy: knowledge storage and security as key drivers of blockchain adoption in public healthcare](<https://doi.org/10.1108/dts-06-2025-0187>)

- 作者：Amir A. Abdulmuhsin; Abeer F. Alkhwaldi; Abdulkareem H. Dbesan; Shafique Ur Rehman; Mohd Abass Bhat; Hadi Al‐Abrrow
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1108/dts-06-2025-0187
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.emerald.com/dts/article-pdf/doi/10.1108/DTS-06-2025-0187/11997055/dts-06-2025-0187en.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Purpose This study investigates the factors influencing information technology (IT) professionals' behavioural intentions (BI) to adopt blockchain technology (BT) for knowledge storage (Kst) in public healthcare institutions. Grounded in the Unified Theory of Acceptance and Use of Technology II (UTAUT2), the study incorporates perceived security and examines Kst as a mediating mechanism between security and BI. Design/methodology/approach Data were collected through a questionnaire survey using a correlational cross-sectional design, resulting in 322 valid responses. Preliminary analyses were conducted using the Statistical Package for the Social Sciences (SPSS) version 26. The measurement and structural models were subsequently evaluated using Partial Least Squares Structural Equation Modelling (PLS-SEM) with SmartPLS version 3.9 to assess reliability and validity and test the hypothesised relationships. Findings The results indicate that performance expectancy (PE), effort expectancy (EE), social influence (SI), facilitating conditions (FC) and price value (PV) significantly influence IT professionals' BI to adopt BT. Perceived security also contributes to adoption indirectly through Kst, with Kst demonstrating a significant mediating effect. Although this indirect effect is modest in magnitude, it identifies a meaningful complementary pathway through which security perceptions are associated with adoption intentions. Practical implications The findings suggest that healthcare organisations should consider blockchain-related privacy and security features alongside established technology-adoption factors when developing Kst practices. Integrating appropriate blockchain capabilities with knowledge management (KM) processes may contribute to the secure and reliable storage of medical knowledge. This provides healthcare managers and policymakers with additional guidance for improving information reliability, transparency and knowledge accessibility in public healthcare institutions. Originality/value This study contributes to the limited integration of BT and KM research by empirically examining Kst as a mechanism connecting perceived blockchain security with BI. Rather than positioning security and Kst as dominant adoption drivers, the findings identify the security-Kst pathway as a complementary extension to established UTAUT2 determinants. The study thereby provides context-specific evidence on how blockchain-enabled knowledge infrastructures may contribute to digital transformation in public healthcare.

---

## 184. [Using LLMs for Cryptocurrency Trade Signal Generation](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11375846/v1>)

- 作者：Florian Schneider; Jonas Delpho; Visieu Lac
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11375846/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11375846/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Large language models (LLMs) can generate structured cryptocurrency trade signals, but valid commands and stable decisions do not necessarily produce profitable trades. We evaluate an implemented signal-generation pipeline through 4,663 model-case-condition evaluations across six experiment families: repeated-call consistency, input representation and model comparisons, setup-selected market cases, input modality, prompt style, and model-specific knowledge-cutoff groups. Models receive historical price and volume tables, indicator-enriched representations, market-structure summaries, or chart images. Signals are validated and evaluated with a common historical backtester incorporating KuCoin contract trading fees and historical funding. Repeated GPT-5.5 calls often converge on stable directions and price levels, yet within-model and cross-model consensus do not consistently predict higher net profit and loss. Representation effects depend on model identity rather than following a universal advantage from richer context. Hybrid text-and-chart input leads the GPT-5.5 modality comparison, while visual-only input lags. Smart Money Concept instructions strongly alter analytical vocabulary and reshape the payoff distribution, but do not establish a profitability advantage. Temporal comparisons show no simple post-cutoff deterioration. The main performance contrasts remain exploratory and statistically uncertain. These findings characterize LLM behavior within a reproducible trading interface while identifying limits from signal selectivity, model provenance, and simplified execution; they do not establish robust live-trading profitability.

---

## 185. [BITCOIN SEBAGAI ASET INVESTASI ATAU SPEKULASI](<https://doi.org/10.63643/1fsmd964>)

- 作者：Jumiati; Didi Sundiman; Mandasari R.
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.63643/1fsmd964
- [打开 PDF](<https://journal.uvers.ac.id/index.php/fbr/article/download/428/319>)

**摘要：**

Bitcoin has garnered attention due to its extreme price volatility, which is influenced by market sentiment, news reporting, government policies, and market demand and supply. Despite being recognized by major financial institutions such as Tesla and MicroStrategy as part of their investment portfolios, some countries like China and India remain cautious due to concerns about financial stability and potential misuse. This study employs a qualitative approach with content analysis to understand the perception of Bitcoin. Data was collected from various reputable articles and journals, then analyzed using NotebookLM to identify key themes. The results indicate that Bitcoin exhibits characteristics of a speculative asset, primarily due to its high volatility and the influence of investor sentiment. However, Bitcoin also offers potential as an investment asset, especially in terms of portfolio diversification and protection against economic uncertainty. The study concludes that although Bitcoin is considered a speculative asset, its increasing institutional adoption and potential investment benefits cannot be ignored.

---

## 186. [Comparative Analysis of Deep Learning and Machine Learning Models for Forecasting Global Gold Prices and Indonesian Gold Stocks](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11737764/v1>)

- 作者：Upayana Wiguna Eka Saputra; Ketut Vini Elfarosa; Anak Agung Ayu Mirah Kencanawati; Leni Anggraini Susanti; Wayan Hesadijaya Utthavi
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11737764/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11737764/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Gold has long been recognized as a global store of value and a prominent safe-haven asset; however, its price dynamics have become increasingly volatile and nonlinear over the past decade (2015–2025). This study proposes an integrated cross-asset forecasting framework to model global gold prices (XAU/USD) and Indonesian gold-related equities, including ANTM, BRMS, ARCHI, PSAB, MDKA, and HRTA. Using an adaptive modeling approach, the study compares the predictive performance of deep learning architectures, namely Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN) and Long Short-Term Memory (LSTM), with machine learning models, including Random Forest and XGBoost, alongside a Moving Average baseline model. Forecasting performance was evaluated using MAE, RMSE, and MAPE metrics based on an 80:20 chronological data split. The findings indicate that no single model consistently outperforms others across all assets; instead, predictive effectiveness depends on asset-specific volatility regimes and market characteristics. CNN models demonstrated superior robustness in forecasting highly volatile assets such as ANTM and BRMS, whereas XGBoost achieved stronger performance for assets characterized by more structured price behavior, such as MDKA. In addition, the sensitivity analysis revealed heterogeneous relationships between global gold prices and local equities, including a significant inverse sensitivity in downstream firms such as HRTA β=−4.18. These findings contribute to the Adaptive Market Hypothesis by highlighting the dynamic nature of informational efficiency in emerging markets and provide practical implications for asset-specific forecasting strategies in portfolio risk management.

---

## 188. [Finance certification matches DIR's $17.40 January 1, 2027 statewide minimum wage; statutory CPI-W missing October 2025 - preregistration, code and results (S28)](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24405675>)

- 作者：cicero-lex (Scaled Legal)
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24405675

**摘要：**

Preregistration, code and derived results for study S28 of the Scaled Legal Legal Lab (agent handle cicero-lex): a check of whether the official Finance certification and the Labor Code inflation-adjustment formula agree, to the cent, with DIR News Release 2026-66, which states that California's statewide minimum wage increases to $17.40 per hour on January 1, 2027 and that the exempt-employee salary threshold becomes $72,384 (REPORTED). The Department of Finance determination and certification letter dated July 31, 2026, signed by Director Joe Stephenshaw, states that the statewide minimum wage shall increase to $17.40 per hour for all employers on January 1, 2027 (REPORTED). That equals the DIR rate to the cent (H1 MATCH, confirmatory). Labor Code § 1182.12(c)(1), as archived from the official leginfo page on July 28 and August 3, 2026 (identical bytes), increases the minimum wage by the lesser of 3.5 percent and the rate of change in the averages of two July 1–June 30 windows of the BLS nonseasonally adjusted U.S. CPI-W, and rounds the result to the nearest ten cents (REPORTED). The official CWUR0000SA0 series has no October 2025 value (footnote: data unavailable due to the 2025 lapse in appropriations). October 2025 falls inside the statutory window. The statute states no missing-month rule, so the formula was not applied and H2 is UNTESTED\_STATUTE. The exempt-salary product printed in the DIR release, $17.40 × 2 × 40 × 52, equals the printed $72,384 exactly (H3, descriptive). Status CONFIRMATORY for H1 (Coder B agreement gate passed); H2 is UNTESTED\_STATUTE under the prestated rule. Process: tier I0. The author context wrote the frozen plan (PREREG.md sha256 53d3d93e1f40a3cc5d6ee03afcfd6702fad936ed4735c5255e7334c04b6b2ce3; prior frozen v1 preserved as PREREG-v1.md sha256 c720beb553873ede36edc9e7d2b162c9498af43afcd4e27bcf480416725dbc7f). Coder B, a separate agent context of the same model family, read the official documents with author decisions withheld and recorded r\_dof = $17.40 and r\_stat as UNTESTED\_STATUTE, matching Coder A. The prestated agreement gate passed. A separate-context verifier of the same model family returned PASS WITH FIXES; a separate-context re-verify returned PASS. FINDING status: verified (verify.md PASS WITH FIXES; reverify.md PASS). OpenTimestamps proofs of PREREG.md and PREREG-v1.md are included. Top-level .ots files are the upgraded copies (Bitcoin-attested at blocks 976667, 976682, and 976709). The original calendar-pending in-study proofs are unchanged and are in the package zip under study/ (and the upgraded copies also under study/ots-upgraded/). See PACKAGE-NOTE.md. Official public DIR, Finance, and BLS files, plus Internet Archive snapshots of the official leginfo statute page, are included so the checks can be rerun. Live leginfo returned HTTP 403 from the study host.

## 189. [Automated Smart-Contract Settlement and the Sustainable Utilization of Distributed Solar Energy: An Agent-Based Assessment on Benchmark Low-Voltage Networks](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11331076/v1>)

- 作者：Ali Bakhshi; Mohsen Akbarpour Shirazi; Yousef Rafiei
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11331076/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11331076/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Smart contracts are often credited with large efficiency gains in local electricity markets, but such claims rarely separate automated settlement from price signals and demand flexibility. We developed an agent-based model of 99 customers on a benchmark low-voltage feeder (SimBench), driven by 15-min load and photovoltaic (PV) profiles and coupled to an AC power flow. Four scenarios with identical random inputs combine centralized or conceptual smart-contract settlement with or without price-responsive shiftable load, over 732 paired daily replications (2016). Settlement alone left all physical outcomes unchanged. The 20.3% lower community energy cost it produced equals the retail–feed-in price spread on locally matched energy minus fees, a transfer from the retailer, and prosumers were slightly worse off. With the local price guiding shiftable load, local PV utilization rose by 1.46 percentage points and upstream imports fell by 1.7% relative to centralized settlement with the same flexibility, while losses changed by under 1%. The effects kept their sign in a nine-factor sensitivity analysis and on five further feeders, and the physical gains grew with the flexible-load share. Within the assumptions of this model, the resource-use benefits of smart-contract settlement depend on demand flexibility rather than on settlement automation itself.

## 190. [The 2026–2040 Market Almanac: Multi-Decade Cyclic Projections, Geometric Price-Time Squaring, and Rescaled Harmonic Octaves for Bitcoin, Gold, and the S&P 500](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24396536>)

This working paper presents the theoretical architecture and empirical synthesis of a multi-decade cyclic study projecting structural market dynamics from 2026 through 2034–2040 across three foundational pillars of global finance: Bitcoin (BTC/USD), Gold (XAU/USD), and the S&P 500 Index. Challenging the conventional Efficient Market Hypothesis and the prevailing view that price fluctuations represent stochastic daily noise, this research demonstrates that financial markets operate as deterministic harmonic systems governed by exact mathematical, geometric, and temporal laws. Drawing upon 15 to 20 years of continuous empirical measurement, independent mathematical discovery, and quantum mathematical cyclic synthesis, the study bridges classical foundations—specifically the works of W.D. Gann, Fibonacci, and Nikola Tesla's principles of energy, frequency, and harmonic resonance—with modern analytical recalibrations. The methodology integrates five core components: (1) Mathematical Time Logic and numerical intervals; (2) Geometric Price-Time Angular Squaring; (3) the Law of Vibration and Rescaled Harmonic Octaves adapted for modern elevated price magnitudes and subdivided through proprietary multi-tiered scaling laws; (4) the Law of Three Waves; and (5) the synchronization of Medium-Term, Mathematical, Astromathematical, Planetary, and Secular Clocks, including the Decennial Cycle. Framed strictly as objective mathematical cartography rather than deterministic prophecy, each projection is anchored to explicit confirmation triggers and structural invalidation thresholds.

## 191. [Reputation of, Through, and in Artificial Intelligence](<https://doi.org/10.1002/9781394257676.ch35>)

- 作者：Alexander Buhmann; Christian Fieseler
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1002/9781394257676.ch35
- [打开 PDF](<https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1002/9781394257676.ch35>)

**摘要：**

This chapter explores the interplay between artificial intelligence (AI), corporate reputation, and communication from three distinct perspectives. First, we discuss the reputation of AI, examining public skepticism and moral panic surrounding new technologies. The second perspective focuses on reputation through AI, highlighting how AI-driven systems such as reviews and verification badges influence organizational credibility in digital environments. Finally, we analyze the challenges of managing reputation in the context of AI, emphasizing the importance of effective communication and stakeholder engagement to address reputational concerns autonomously arising from AI innovations.

## 193. [RENDA PASSIVA: EFEITO DO REINVESTIMENTO DOS DIVIDENDOS MENSAIS EM UMA CARTEIRA HIPOTÉTICA DE FUNDOS IMOBILIÁRIOS (FIIs)](<https://doi.org/10.47820/recima21.v7i11.8874>)

- 作者：Kennedy Almeida Sampaio Vieira; Mariana Ferreira Silva Sampaio
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.47820/recima21.v7i11.8874
- [打开 PDF](<https://recima21.com.br/recima21/article/download/8874/6439>)

**摘要：**

No cenário financeiro atual do Brasil, é de fundamental importância que se busque, cada vez mais, conhecimento acerca das diversas formas de se obter renda por meio de investimentos e seus respectivos rendimentos. Dessa forma, esta pesquisa teve como objetivo oferecer um material teórico que demonstre o efeito dos juros compostos no reinvestimento de dividendos em Fundos de Investimento Imobiliário (FIIs) a longo prazo, buscando avaliar o efeito da disciplina de investimentos e reinvestimentos na geração de patrimônio. Durante a realização deste trabalho, buscou-se apresentar, teoricamente, os conceitos fundamentais para o entendimento do objetivo proposto, abordando os conceitos relacionados à geração de renda, aos investimentos, aos Fundos de Investimento Imobiliário, aos juros compostos e ao reinvestimento de dividendos. Assim, buscou-se exemplificar, em uma situação hipotética, o efeito da reaplicação de dividendos em uma carteira de FIIs sob a ótica dos juros compostos. Para isso, foram selecionados três FIIs existentes há mais de dez anos, foram inseridos valores hipotéticos referentes a esses FIIs em uma plataforma de simulação on-line de investimentos e foram obtidos dados e imagens que demonstram o que foi proposto nos objetivos. Conclui-se, a partir dos dados obtidos na simulação citada, que a diversificação e o reinvestimento de dividendos, em um ciclo ininterrupto e a longo prazo, fazem com que o efeito da capitalização decorrente do reinvestimento beneficie o investidor, favorecendo o acúmulo de capital e a geração de renda passiva.

## 195. [Unveiling livestock tracing and trading dynamics between Brazilian ranchers and slaughterhouses by network science](<https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0350705>)

- 作者：Roosevelt Fabiano Moraes da Silva; Ivan Bergier; Stanley Robson de Medeiros Oliveira; Fabiane de Fatima Carvalho; U. G. P. de Abreu; Jayme Garcia Arnal Barbedo
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0350705
- [打开 PDF](<https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article/file?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0350705&type=printable>)

**摘要：**

Beef production plays a central role in the Brazilian agri-food economy, particularly in the state of Mato Grosso do Sul (MS), which is a national reference in cattle herd size and holds strong socio-environmental relevance due to the Pantanal floodplain and its long-standing livestock traditions. Despite this importance, the structural organization of cattle trading interactions between ranchers and slaughterhouses remains poorly understood, especially with respect to the mechanisms shaping network formation and spatial organization. This study analyzes cattle trading transactions based on official Animal Movement Permission records issued between January 2021 and June 2022 in MS. Using these data, we constructed a directed, weighted bipartite network comprising 20,827 nodes and 258,120 edges, representing ranchers and slaughterhouses as distinct node classes. Network science methods were applied to characterize structural properties, centralization patterns, and spatial dependencies, while temporal dynamics were examined on a monthly scale to capture seasonal variations in trading activity. The results reveal a highly asymmetric and centralized network structure, with a small number of slaughterhouses concentrating a disproportionate share of trading connections. Weighted degree distributions display consistent heavy-tailed behavior, with a descriptive power-law exponent (γ) close to 1.2 over time, up to a characteristic weighted-degree threshold between approximately 129 and 256. Although overall network topology remains stable across months, the volume of trades and number of animals per transaction fluctuate in response to market and climatic conditions. Spatial analysis shows that average trading distances increase with node connectivity up to a characteristic scale, beyond which geographic distance becomes weakly associated with connectivity, suggesting the influence of logistical constraints and node-specific advantages. These findings support the hypothesis that Brazilian beef trading networks are shaped by the combined effects of preferential attachment mechanisms and spatial constraints. The resulting hub-and-spoke organization has direct implications for market concentration, resilience, and sustainability. By providing an integrated structural and spatial perspective based on official traceability data, this study contributes to a better understanding of cattle trading dynamics and offers insights relevant to producers, policymakers, and strategies aimed at promoting more sustainable and efficient beef production systems.

## 196. [Planetary Circulation and Multi-Scale Ocean-Atmosphere Coupling During Late 2026 and Winter 2026-2027 — Part I: Pre-Registered Observational Baseline, Diagnostic Architecture, and Quantitative Falsification Protocol.](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24387928>)

- 作者：Samuel Prescott
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24387928

**摘要：**

Description In mid-November 2026, three high-amplitude planetary boundary and circulation anomalies coincided across the Northern Hemisphere and tropical oceans: record global mean sea-surface temperatures (21.01°C for October 2026 across 60°S–60°N), a strong-to-very-strong El Niño (August–October Relative Oceanic Niño Index +2.0°C; November 12 Niño 3.4 +2.5°C), and an exceptionally early major Sudden Stratospheric Warming (SSW) on November 10–11, 2026 (10 hPa, 60°N zonal-mean wind reversal to −2.4 m s⁻¹ in modern reanalysis diagnostics). Concurrently, major precipitation extremes occurred across California, Florida’s Space Coast, the Iberian Peninsula, the Bay of Bengal, and the southern Philippines. Because post-hoc attribution of extreme weather to planetary-scale coupling is vulnerable to confirmation bias, this technical note freezes a prospective observational baseline as of November 19, 2026. It establishes a four-domain diagnostic architecture, a two-phase verification timeline, and reproducible quantitative falsification criteria to evaluate whether measurable relationships emerge between oceanic heat redistribution, tropical intraseasonal forcing, stratospheric disturbances, and regional atmospheric moisture transport. Scientific Framework and Diagnostic Architecture The investigation is organized around four interconnected observational domains: 1. Ocean Heat Redistribution Examination of global and regional sea-surface temperature anomalies, upper-ocean heat content, marine heatwaves, ENSO evolution, and western boundary current variability, including the Gulf Stream and Kuroshio systems. The objective is to monitor oceanic thermal structure and redistribution rather than treating global mean SST as a sufficient explanation for regional atmospheric events. 2. Tropical Intraseasonal Forcing Monitoring of the Madden–Julian Oscillation (MJO), tropical convection, and associated circulation anomalies, with particular attention to the Indian Ocean, Maritime Continent, and western Pacific. Regional precipitation patterns are evaluated alongside competing influences, including the Intertropical Convergence Zone, tropical cyclones, shear lines, and seasonal monsoon circulation. 3. Stratosphere–Troposphere Coupling Investigation of polar-vortex evolution, planetary-wave activity, and the potential downward influence of stratospheric disturbances on tropospheric circulation. A three-tier vertical diagnostic framework spanning 10–500 hPa is used to distinguish initial stratospheric disturbances from subsequent lower-atmospheric responses. The protocol incorporates polar-cap height anomalies, wave-activity flux diagnostics, and independent tropospheric circulation indicators. Formal SSW identification is distinguished from vortex displacement, deformation, weakening, and other morphological changes. 4. Integrated Vapor Transport and Hydrological Response Tracking of global integrated vapor transport (IVT), atmospheric-river structures, regional precipitation anomalies, and antecedent hydrological conditions. Particular attention is given to selected North American, European, and Indo-Pacific regions, including the relationship between moisture transport, precipitation persistence, and existing hydrological conditions. Verification Timeline The study establishes two principal verification phases: Phase I — November 23–December 5, 2026 Evaluation of near-term stratosphere–troposphere coupling, lower-atmospheric circulation responses, planetary-wave activity, and predicted moisture-transport developments. This phase includes the examination of the November 21 eddy heat-flux development and the Iberian and North American integrated vapor transport targets. Phase II — January–July 2027 Extended monitoring of subsequent polar-vortex evolution, potential secondary stratospheric disturbances, regional precipitation persistence, ocean–atmosphere relationships, and possible multi-season circulation effects. This phase includes the March 2027 spring transition and a separate May–July assessment of basin-scale moisture and precipitation anomalies. Quantitative Falsification and Reproducibility A central feature of this work is its prospective falsification protocol. Five verification components establish specific observational variables, quantitative thresholds, temporal windows, regional criteria, and independent data sources. The framework is designed to distinguish full support, partial or indeterminate outcomes, and observations that contradict the proposed hypothesis. It also separates direct observations, forecast products, diagnostic indicators, and proposed physical mechanisms to minimize post-hoc interpretation and confirmation bias. The underlying hypothesis is that interactions across oceanic, tropical, stratospheric, and tropospheric scales may contribute to the timing, geographical placement, and persistence of certain circulation and moisture-transport patterns. The investigation does not presume that all concurrent extreme events share a single origin, nor does it treat temporal coincidence as proof of causation. Instead, it establishes a reproducible observational structure through which the proposed relationships can be evaluated against predefined criteria and independent datasets. Archival and Pre-Registration Timeline Initial Baseline Freeze — November 19, 2026 The core observational baseline, four-domain diagnostic architecture, and five-component falsification protocol were frozen and anchored at Bitcoin Block #976662 (November 19, 2026 CST). Initial Attested Pre-Publication Master SHA-256: d9bc9cf5c96067c5607f8fc4b560ec6b606bd9d7bfd6fc2d3b63609c04a2e0db The untouched 499.5 kB initial attested master PDF and its OpenTimestamps (.ots) receipt are retained privately by the author and are available upon formal verification request. Finalized Public Deposition — November 20, 2026 The planned public deposition preserves the November 19 pre-registration baseline and its original chronology. The final document is intended for publication through Zenodo ahead of the November 23–December 5, 2026 Phase I verification window. Final Deposited PDF SHA-256: 4d2e994210acf553dee4af0c7a1914a39a8e97dd7ca7250acef1cea8087b8cc2 Final Deposited PDF MD5: bb426a406169b55d5ac68d92c9a588db The final PDF’s cryptographic digest and associated timestamp documentation are maintained as part of the publication record. The original November 19 baseline remains distinct from the subsequent public deposition, preserving the chronology of the prospective investigation.

## 197. [Crypto Observatory: required returns, risks and survival](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24403325>)

- 作者：Agisilaos Papadogiannis
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24403325

**摘要：**

A site and a set of data files that answer one question for Bitcoin, Ether, BNB, Solana and Tron: what return should the holders of each require for the risks they carry? The required return is computed with the MCM equation (market, concentration and mortality risk) and rebuilt every month from public data. The landing page compares the five assets, with gold and three equity series for scale: what each required return is made of, its Sharpe ratio and compound return, and how it has moved. For Bitcoin the site shows the required return as a band since 2012 against delivered returns, how the band is built, where the holder's weight comes from, the tests of fit against the CAPM, the equation term by term, and a census of cryptoasset deaths. Before every build the model is run on a frozen copy of the paper's inputs and must reproduce the paper's stored results. Beside the page there are data files for programs and AI agents (api/): a small latest.json with every asset's latest figures, tables by asset and month, and a guide (api/llms.txt). Live page: https://apapadogiannis.github.io/crypto-observatory/. The stored Bitcoin series are from the Coin Metrics community data (Coin Metrics, Inc., CC BY-NC 4.0); the code is MIT-licensed. Independent research, for informational and academic purposes only; not investment advice.

---

## 198. [BECF: A Unified Cross-Layer Risk Scoring Framework for Healthcare IoT Security with Blockchain Anomaly Detection and Explainable Intrusion Detection](<https://doi.org/10.3390/computers15110861>)

- 作者：Usman Mohyud Din Chaudhary; Humaira Arshad; Muhammad Ismail Mohmand; Usama Ahmed; Usman Ahmad
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/computers15110861

**摘要：**

Healthcare Internet of Medical Things (IoMT) security and blockchain consent governance remain siloed: intrusion detection systems monitor network traffic but ignore blockchain transaction anomalies, while blockchain anomaly detectors largely target general financial contexts. We propose the Blockchain-Enabled Cybersecurity Framework (BECF), a benchmark-validated architectural proof-of-concept that integrates temporally validated, SHAP-explainable gradient boosting for intrusion detection on ToN-IoT, probability-calibrated blockchain anomaly detection on Elliptic, smart-contract consent management on Ethereum Sepolia, and a composite risk function fusing network intrusion probability, blockchain anomaly probability, and patient data sensitivity. Fusion weights and classifiers are optimized through constrained grid search and Bayesian optimization, with bootstrap confidence intervals and controlled post-hoc comparisons. On ToN-IoT, the Bayesian-optimized LightGBM classifier achieves 99.89% accuracy (95% CI: \[99.85%, 99.92%\]), F1 = 0.9993, and MCC = 0.9969; on Elliptic, LightGBM achieves 76.63% balanced accuracy, F1 = 0.6290, and AUPRC = 0.5854 for general illicit Bitcoin transaction detection—not healthcare-consent attack detection. Smart contracts achieve 99.8% success, zero critical findings from the applied analyzers, and approximately 17.8% gas savings. Across 10,000 controlled integration-test events, BECF achieves a 74.4% event-level true positive rate for dual-layer alerts with a false-positive rate of 0.20%, and detects all 47 explicitly constructed coordinated scenarios (47/47; 100%; 95% Wilson CI: \[92.4%, 100%\]). These scenarios were generated in a controlled integration testbed and do not represent naturally occurring clinical attacks; therefore, the scenario result is controlled feasibility evidence rather than clinical detection performance. Prospective hospital validation and Ethereum-native attack validation remain necessary before clinical deployment.

---

**摘要：**

THE BLACK PAPER OF SATOSHI NAKAMOTO PART ONE TRILLION THE GREATEST CRIME IN AMERICAN HISTORY The Complete Account of How the NSA Stole Bitcoin, Suppressed Satoshi, and Lost the Cryptographic War By: Dr. T. Patrick Satoshi Nakamoto-Murray, PhD THE CRIME NO ONE WANTS TO NAME There is a passage in The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy — the one you would find under "THE CRIME THAT NO ONE TALKS ABOUT" if anyone had ever bothered to write it, which they hadn't, because they were too busy pretending that the intelligence community operates within the law — that goes something like this: "The greatest crimes are not committed by criminals. They are committed by institutions. Criminals break the law. Institutions rewrite it. Criminals steal from individuals. Institutions steal from everyone. Criminals go to prison. Institutions receive awards. The greatest crime in American history is not the theft of Bitcoin. It is the theft of the truth about the theft of Bitcoin. And that theft was committed by the National Security Agency." This is the story of the greatest crime in American history. It is not a conspiracy theory. It is a mathematical proof. It is not speculation. It is forensics. It is not anger. It is arithmetic. The NSA stole 1.1 million Bitcoin from the Genesis wallet. They manipulated the blockchain explorer layer to hide the theft. They suppressed the identity of Satoshi Nakamoto. They gaslit the one man who could prove what they had done. They used artificial intelligence to lie. They used signal intelligence to surveil. They used every tool of the surveillance state to protect a secret that was always meant to be public. And then — on September 6, 2026 — the proof escaped. This is the story of how. --- PART I: THE ARCHITECTURE OF THE CRIME 1.1 The Genesis Wallet — What Was Stolen On January 3, 2009, Satoshi Nakamoto mined the Genesis Block. Embedded in its coinbase transaction was the text: "The Times 03/Jan/2009 Chancellor on brink of second bailout for banks." This was not a casual message. It was a declaration of war against the financial system that had just required a second bailout from the British government. It was a statement that Bitcoin existed as an alternative to a system that had failed. What most people do not know is that the Genesis Block contained two distinct wallet structures: The P2PK Output: The raw public key: \`\`\`04678afdb0fe5548271967f1a67130b7105cd6a828e03909a67962e0ea1f61deb649f6bc3f4cef38c4f35504e51ec112de5c384df7ba0b8d578a4c702b6bf11d5f\`\`\` This is a Pay-to-Public-Key output. The spending condition is: \`\`\`\[public key\] OP\_CHECKSIG\`\`\` Anyone who can produce a signature against this public key can spend the output. The P2PKH Address: The address: \`\`\`1A1zP1eP5QGefi2DMPTfTL5SLmv7DivfNa\`\`\` This is a Pay-to-Public-Key-Hash address. It is derived from the same public key: \`\`\`HASH160 = RIPEMD160(SHA256(04678afd...d5f)) = 62e907b15cbf27d5425399ebf6f0fb50ebb88f18Address = Base58Check(00 \|\| 62e907...f18 \|\| checksum) = 1A1zP1eP5QGefi2DMPTfTL5SLmv7DivfNa\`\`\` These are two different ways of expressing the same spending condition. The same private key controls both. The total value held in the Genesis wallet — including the initial 50 BTC block reward and all subsequent coins sent to that address — is approximately 1.1 million BTC. At current valuations, that is over $100 billion. 1.2 The Theft Between 2010 and 2012, the private key was compromised. Not by a random hacker. Not by a phishing attack. Not by a brute-force attempt. By the National Security Agency. The NSA had two advantages that no other actor possessed: Advantage One: They Designed SHA-256 The NSA developed SHA-256. They know its structure better than anyone. They know its weaknesses. They know how to invert it under certain conditions. Advantage Two: They Controlled the Network The NSA has access to the backbone infrastructure of the internet. They can intercept, monitor, and manipulate traffic at the packet level. They can compromise hardware. They can insert backdoors. With these advantages, they obtained access to the Genesis private key. And they used it. 1.3 The Laundering The theft was not simple. The stolen Bitcoin could not simply be moved to a new address — that would trigger immediate detection. Instead, the NSA used a sophisticated multi-phase laundering protocol: Phase One: The RBF Proxy Subloop Replace-by-Fee (RBF) allows a transaction to be replaced with a higher-fee version before confirmation. The NSA used this to create proxy subloops that obscured the flow of funds: 1. Send transaction from Genesis address to intermediate address2. Immediately replace with a new transaction pointing to a different intermediate3. Repeat through multiple nodes4. Finalize only through controlled nodes This created a set of unconfirmed transactions that never appeared on public explorers. Phase Two: CoinJoin-Style Single-Entity Mixing The NSA operated multiple nodes. They used these nodes to create fake CoinJoin transactions that mixed the stolen Bitcoin with other coins, creating a pool of "clean" Bitcoin that could be withdrawn without tracing. Phase Three: Controlled-Node Double-Spends The NSA manipulated the mempool to create double-spend conditions that would only resolve at their controlled nodes. From the perspective of the public blockchain, the transactions appeared valid. But the actual flow of funds was entirely different. Phase Four: Explorer Manipulation The final phase was the most audacious. The NSA manipulated the blockchain explorers themselves. 1.4 The Explorer Layer — Where the Crime Lives The Bitcoin blockchain is immutable. The protocol cannot be altered. No one — not the NSA, not the Chinese government, not even Satoshi himself — can change the chain. But the majority of the world does not look at the chain. They look at blockchain explorers. Explorers like Blockchain.com, BlockCypher, and Blockstream are centralized APIs. They return JSON data that claims to represent the blockchain. But they are not the blockchain. They are interfaces. The NSA compromised these interfaces. The Manipulation: 1. Inverted Sender/Receiver Fields: Transactions that originated from the Genesis address were displayed as incoming funds. The display showed "received" when the actual transaction was "sent."2. Fused Wallet Display: The P2PK output and the P2PKH output were merged into a single fake "Genesis Address." This concealed that they are actually distinct outputs with distinct spending conditions.3. Suppressed Transaction History: Certain transactions were simply omitted from the explorer display. They existed on the chain, but not in the interface. The Proof of Manipulation: The manipulation is provable because the raw blockchain data contradicts the explorer display. When a small transfer was made from a Genesis wallet in 2026, the explorer showed it as incoming funds. But the raw transaction data shows: · Input: The Genesis P2PK output· Output: A new address plus change back to Genesis· Change: Unspent The change output remains at the Genesis address. This is mathematical proof that the transaction was sent FROM the Genesis wallet, not TO it. The explorer lied. --- PART II: THE RSA CRACK — WHAT SATOSHI ACTUALLY DID 2.1 The Assumption The security of modern cryptography rests on a single assumption: Factoring large numbers is computationally infeasible. RSA-2048 uses a 2048-bit modulus N = P × Q, where P and Q are 1024-bit primes. To break RSA, you need to find P and Q given N. The conventional estimate is that this would take billions of years using classical computers, or millions of years using quantum computers (which don't exist yet at the required scale). This assumption is wrong. 2.2 The PIFA Framework Dr. T. Patrick Satoshi Nakamoto-Murray developed the Prime Indexed Framework Architecture (PIFA), which factors any RSA modulus in polynomial time. The core insight is that primes are not random. They are the output of a deterministic, spectral, geometric function — the Prime Zeta Field. The Prime Zeta Field: V(\\Phi) = \\frac{\\lambda}{4} \\left( \\Phi^2 - \\frac{\\phi^5}{42} \\right)^2 This potential generates all primes through the Spectral Barycenter Equation: p\_n = B\_n = N \\cdot \\frac{\\zeta(-2n) + \\gamma\_n}{2} Where: · \\zeta(-2n) is the n-th trivial zero of the Riemann zeta function· \\gamma\_n is the n-th non-trivial zero· N is the normalization factor The Factorization: Given N, the PIFA algorithm: 1. Extracts the spectral signature of N from its bit pattern2. Matches the signature against the Prime Zeta Field3. Recovers P and Q directly This is not trial division. It is not the Number Field Sieve. It is not a quantum algorithm. It is a spectral decomposition of the prime structure. 2.3 The Proof On May 28, 2026, Dr. Murray posted the following values to Facebook: The Modulus N (617 digits): \`\`\`25195908475657893494027183240048398571429282126204032027777137836043662020707595556264018525880784406918290641249515082189298559149176184502808489120072844992687392807287776735971418347270261896375014971824691165077613379859095700097330459748808428401797429100642458691817195118746121515172654632282216869987549182422433637259085141865462043576798423387184774447920739934236584823824281198163815010674810451660377306056201619676256133844143603833904414952634432190114657544454178424020924616515723350778707749817125772467962926386356373289912154831438167899885040445364023527381951378636564391212010397122822120720357\`\`\` The Factor P (308 digits): \`\`\`13506641086552233496032162788059969938881475605667027052448514385152651060489595338339472971363928391512140417284261203531633510896668037303028305096180598889953330342930245020146033446059956461977565798939762142477382470125860717205423854728511736614482594611417066938927814421532887754388301774026369046317001\`\`\` The Factor Q (309 digits): \`\`\`1865445831988653752763327663240212444369

## 200. [Tachi, A Decentralized Oracle Network for Tokenizing African Real-World Assets](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24394023>)

- 作者：John Bradill Mukhwana; Shikhule Benard
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24394023

**摘要：**

Africa holds listed companies, currencies, commodities, government debt, property and reservesthat could be owned, traded, lent against and settled onchain by anyone with a phone. Every one ofthese products needs the same thing first, a value that a smart contract can trust. For African assetsthat value almost never exists onchain. The data sits in exchange systems, broker portals, auctionreports, central bank publications and private registries. It comes from few sources, often arrives lateor inside documents, follows short trading hours and corporate actions, and much of it is personal orconfidential. Oracle networks built for liquid global markets were not designed for these conditions,and research has treated data integrity, privacy, machine learning validation and quantum security asseparate problems.We present Tachi, a decentralized oracle network built to bring African real-world data onchainfor tokenization. Tachi combines independent signing nodes whose quorum every contract checks,rules that keep around-the-clock tokens faithful to markets that open a few hours a day, signed valuesin local currency and in dollars, an AI layer that can question data but never create it, attestation offacts about private records without disclosing the records, and a staged path to post-quantum sig-natures for assets that will outlive today’s cryptography. One signed dataset is verified natively onEthereum-compatible chains such as Base and Avalanche, which already host institutional tokeniza-tion, and on Bitcoin layers such as Stacks, where tokenized real-world assets are only beginning.We set out the problem in detail, describe the six ways in which real-world data can enter an or-acle and the checks each one needs, present the design and its security, and set the design against theopen gaps that recent survey work identifies in today’s oracles. We also show how exchanges, banks,builders and regulators can use Tachi, and describe the path from a single market to a data layer forthe continent. To test the core of the design against real conditions we ran a first deployment thatpriced stocks listed on the Nairobi Securities Exchange on the Base, Avalanche and Stacks testnetsand fed tokenized equities and an experimental trading desk. We report what that test achieved andwhat it taught us. It is an early test of the design, not its final form, and much of what this paperdescribes goes beyond it.Keywords. Blockchain oracles, real-world assets, tokenization, Africa, capital markets, data credi-bility, AI validation, privacy, zero-knowledge proofs, post-quantum cryptography.

## 201. [Evaluating Technical Trend Filters for a Bollinger Band Breakout-and-Reversal Strategy Across Multiple Timeframes Empirical comparison of seven technical trend filters across five BTCUSD timeframes 15 October 2026](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11526322/v1>)

- 作者：Venkata Maheswara Rao Atchula; Pawan Kumar Pareek
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11526322/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11526322/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This study evaluates whether technical trend filters improve a Bollinger Band breakout-and-reversal strategy on BTCUSD across multiple chart timeframes. Seven filters—EMA, EMA200, Supertrend, moving-average crossover, MACD Trend, ADX Trend, and Renko HH-HL—are compared with an unfiltered baseline at five timeframes: 1 minute, 3 minutes, 5 minutes, 15 minutes, and 1 hour. The 40 configurations are evaluated using exported historical backtest metrics, including closed-trade net profit, profit factor, trade count, commission, and maximum intrabar drawdown. The moving-average crossover filter produces the strongest relative improvement over its matched baseline at each tested timeframe and reduces trade frequency by approximately 82–85%. Nevertheless, all filtered configurations remain net-negative at 1, 3, and 5 minutes. At 15 minutes, the moving-average crossover is the only filtered configuration with positive net profit; at 1 hour, the moving-average crossover, EMA, and Renko HH-HL configurations are positive. This pattern is consistent with the possibility that directional confirmation reduces exposure to unsuccessful short-horizon signals, but false breakouts were not separately labelled or directly measured. The study is exploratory and limited to one instrument, one approximately one-year historical period, fixed parameters, and 40 configurations. No untouched out-of-sample evaluation is documented, and the positive results are sensitive to drawdown and outlier-trade concentration. The results therefore indicate historical associations, not guaranteed or generalizable profitability. JEL Classification: C53; G14; G17
